# Phase 02 — task ledger

One row per task. **Status values:** `DONE` (implemented and verified by something executed) ·
`IN PROGRESS` · `TODO` · `BLOCKED` (a genuine external blocker, named) · `NOT STARTED`.

`DONE` never means "written". It means a command was run and its output is recorded in
[`PHASE-02-TEST-EVIDENCE.md`](PHASE-02-TEST-EVIDENCE.md).

Decision IDs follow the Master Product Plan / Decision Register v1.0 (2026-10-14). Where a task
implements a design decision this repository made rather than one the register fixes, the design
document is cited instead — inventing a register ID would be worse than citing nothing.

---

Found by reading the card in the running application rather than counting tables; the fixture now gives the orders overlapping product lists and the dashboard renders 5 ranked rows. 4 tests / 16 assertions pin it: the block lists exactly the products the orders contain and no others, a foreign product whose id one of this store's orders names does not appear (the `(clone $productQuery)` intersection is the tenant control — `Product` registers no `StoreScope` and is absent from `scoped_models`), a staff member holding `Manage Order` but not `Manage Products` is not shown it, and with no orders it renders its own empty state. **Bitten three ways** — `Product::whereIn()` in place of the scoped clone → the foreign product appears; the permission gate removed → staff see the table; `pluck('product_json')` → the block empties and 2 cases fail | — |

Found by driving `/dashboard` in the running application; the preview harness supplies `$GRANTS` directly and never resolves a name. Ratchet reads grantable names from the seeders and bites. | — |

| P2-07-6 | Status honesty pass in the app | PRD-001 | **DONE for the app's own half; the API half is F29 and escalated** | `lib/view/new_widgets/order_status_badge.dart`, `lib/view/new_widgets/payment_status_bottom_sheet.dart`, `lib/translations/{en,ar}.dart`, `test/widgets/order_status_label_test.dart` | **FOUR DEFECTS, all presentational.** (1) statuses **7 and 8 rendered "unknown"** — the switch handled 0..6 while `orders.delivered_status` carries nine values, and `'unknown'` had no key in either translation file so GetX returned the raw lowercase word; (2) status 2 asked for `'cancel'` (the BUTTON word, "Cancel"/"إلغاء") while `'cancelled'` ("Cancelled"/"ملغى") sat unused in both files — the same wrong-sense class as the web's "Stock"→"الأسهم"; (3) `getOrderDescription` was **nine hardcoded Arabic literals with no `.tr`**, so an English merchant read them in Arabic; (4) **three badge colour pairs below AA** — status 0 at 2.81:1, status 5 at 4.06:1 and **status 6 at 1.85:1** (a pale amber word on a pale amber chip). Hardcoded pairs inside the widget, so no theme check was ever going to reach them. Hue kept, text darkened to 5.75 / 6.60 / 5.86:1; 7 and 8 get indigo and brown, outside the warm-to-green progression, having previously fallen through to the same grey a corrupt value gets. Also: the status sheet wrote `getOrderName`'s hardcoded ENGLISH into `model.orderStatusText`, which the detail screen displays and the server sends already translated — so an Arabic merchant's status line flipped to English the moment they changed the status. **27 tests; 1222 pass; analyze 246 issues / 0 errors / 0 warnings. Bite-tested, and one bite found a gap in the TEST rather than the code:** reverting `'cancelled'` to `'cancel'` left everything green because both keys resolve to real words, so two cases were added comparing each label to the VALUE OF THE KEY it should use — a key swap now fails while a wording change does not. | F29 for the API half |

## Slice P2-04 — orders (Workstream E)

| ID | Task | Decisions | Status | Files | Verified by | Blocker |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2-04-1 | Make the order-status breakdown **partition** the total | ORD | **DONE** | `HomeController.php`, `dashboard/overview.blade.php` | browser checks read the rendered chips and the total off the page and compare; **bites** (old behaviour shows 1,272 against a total of 1,284) | — |
| P2-04-2 | Name the two values that are not fulfilment states | ORD | **DONE** | `overview.blade.php` | browser check asserts the wording and both labels | — |
| P2-04-3 | Status constants on `HomeController`, with the stale migration comment recorded | — | **DONE** | `HomeController.php` | 9 values confirmed live by grep across `app/` and `resources/` | — |
| P2-04-4 | Order list: design-system adoption | UX-001, F8/F9 | **DONE** | `app/DataTables/OrderDataTable.php`, `app/Support/OrderStatusTone.php`, `resources/views/order/action.blade.php`, `public/css/custom.css`, `public/css/rtl-custom.css`, `docs/development/phase-2/tools/check-row-actions-live.cjs` | **The status column and the row actions came off the vendored theme's classes.** Measured in the running application with WCAG alpha compositing across LTR/RTL x light/dark: the status buttons were below AA in **44 of 56 readings**, two of them unreadable — `btn-soft-dark` (Shipped, Partially Paid, Pre Order) at **1.12:1** in dark mode and `btn-soft-danger` (Cancel, Return) at **1.03:1**; `btn-soft-success` 2.59:1 everywhere, `btn-soft-warning` 1.68:1 in light. Now `ss-badge`, worst reading **6.82:1**. The tone comes from a single `OrderStatusTone`, which the dashboard reads too: the two surfaces had **disagreed about four of the nine statuses** (Shipped `info` there / `dark` here, Confirmed `info` / `warning`, Pending `warning` / `info`, Picked Up `info` / `secondary`), so one order changed colour between two screens a merchant opens one after the other. Row actions: icons 1.09:1 → 5.94:1 and up, and the order-reference pill 2.59:1 → 6.92:1 via F9's own `--ss-onbrand` (no brand colour changed, no new policy, nothing escalated). 84 live cases in `check-row-actions-live.cjs`, **bitten five ways** — the font-less icon name → 4 failures naming it; one `aria-label` dropped → 4 failures; the `--ss-onbrand` rule reverted → 2.59:1 on `#ORD-2047`; one badge tone's colour broken → 1:1 on Cancel; and `--ss-success-fg` broken → 8 failures across BOTH lists, which is what shows the product stock badges are covered. Plus Unit 370, and the order suites 18/18 | — |
| P2-04-4a | F19 — the list no longer renders the Unix epoch as a date | — | **DONE** | `app/DataTables/OrderDataTable.php` | `OrderStatusDateTest` — 4 cases through the real yajra AJAX leg. One root cause: `Utility::dateFormat()` is `date($fmt, strtotime($date))`, `strtotime(null)` is FALSE, and `date($fmt, false)` is the epoch — so an order marked Delivered with no `delivery_date` read **"Delivered : Jan 1, 1970"**, a fabricated date in the same type as the real ones beside it. The Date column did the same for a null `order_date`. Now: '-' for the date column, and a badge keeps its LABEL and drops only the date half, because the status is what the column exists for. `hasDate()` also rejects `'0000-00-00'`. Bite-tested: short-circuiting `hasDate()` fails exactly the two epoch cases. **Confirmed in the running application** — the rendered Arabic order table contains no `1970`. | — |
| P2-04-5 | Quick order preview in a side panel | UX | **DONE** | `OrderController::order_preview`, `order/partials/preview.blade.php`, `routes/web.php`, `OrderDocumentAccessGuard::TARGETS`, `public/js/store-syria-ds.js` | `OrderPreviewAuthorizationTest` — 9 cases, cross-tenant, per-permission, anonymous, non-integer ids, and **the two controls measured separately**: removing the handler's own check fails exactly the direct-call case, removing the guard's TARGETS entry fails exactly the registration case. Driven in a real browser across 8 combinations. The id is RAW, deliberately: `Crypt::encrypt` is an encoding here, not a control. | — |
| P2-04-5-OLD | (superseded row) | UX | — | — | — | Needs a NEW read-only route, and the brief requires it be restricted to authorized merchant data. It must therefore carry its own `store_id` predicate derived from the authenticated principal — it cannot lean on `Order::order_detail()`, which has none (see P2-04-6). A new route is also the one place a tenant predicate can be got right from scratch rather than inherited. |

Status, payment status and a date range. **THE STATUS OPTIONS ARE BUILT FROM `HomeController`'s CONSTANTS**, not typed out — the column holds NINE values and 7 (Partially Paid) and 8 (Pre Order) are not fulfilment states, so a hand-written 0–6 list would have made those orders unreachable from the one screen a merchant uses to find them. They are offered in their own labelled group, repeating the dashboard's honesty. **THE DATE RANGE IS THE HALF WITH THE SHARP EDGES:** the end date is `endOfDay()`, because `order_date` is a datetime and a naive `<= date` compares against MIDNIGHT and silently drops the day the merchant typed (the fixture puts an order at 23:45 on it for exactly that); the parse is `createFromFormat('Y-m-d')` with a round-trip check, not `Carbon::parse`, which accepts `'0'`, `'now'` and `'+1 day'` and throws on nonsense. Payment is an EXACT match, never a `LIKE` — "Paid" and "Partially Paid" are different financial states. `OrderListFilterTest` — 14 cases. **Five controls bite-tested separately:** `endOfDay()` → 1 fail, payment as `LIKE` → 1, statuses limited to 0–6 → 1, strict parse → `Carbon::parse` → 1, store predicate → 1. | P2-04-14 | WS E11 — **RULE: order products and quantities cannot be edited after creation** <br><br>**Acceptance:** a server-side guard, not a hidden control; an attempt is refused and logged | AUDIT-2026-10-25; **commerce-integrity rule stated by the owner, tracked by nothing before this audit** | **DONE [2026-10-26]** | `tests/Feature/Security/OrderLineImmutabilityTest.php` (9 tests), `tests/Feature/Security/OrderStatusUnknownValueTest.php` (7 tests), `app/Models/Order.php` (the F81 default return), `resources/lang/{en,ar}.json` | **THE RULE HOLDS, AND IT IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN READ.** There is no line-item table: `orders.product_json` holds the lines, `orders.product_id` a comma list and `orders.product_price` their subtotal -- **and all three are in `Order::$fillable`**, which is what makes the question real. A census established three things before any test was written: no order-mutating route accepts a product list; every handler assigns the ONE named field it is about (`$order->payment_status = $request->input(...)`), never the request wholesale; and the only `$order->update([...])` in the codebase (`OrderRefundController:359`) passes a literal array. The two post-creation writers of `product_json` are legitimate and neither changes products or quantities -- `Order::product_return()` adds the P1-INV-05 restock marker, and `StoreCurrencyConversionService` rewrites PRICES on a currency change, which is E14's question not E11's. **THE ACCEPTANCE ASKS FOR A SERVER-SIDE GUARD, NOT A HIDDEN CONTROL, so the test posts the edit directly** -- `product_json`, `product_id`, `product_price`, `qty`, `quantity` and a `products` array -- at all **six** order-mutating endpoints, and reads the row back. Plus a RATCHET: no handler may `update`/`fill`/`forceFill` an order from `$request`, which is the one change that would break every case at once and is an easy thing to write while adding an endpoint. **Bitten twice:** making one handler apply the posted `product_json` fails that endpoint's case and the sequence case while the other five still pass; introducing a mass-assignment fails the ratchet. **THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS TEST WAS HALF VACUOUS, and the fix is the useful part of this row.** It posted a guessed payload and asserted only that the lines were unchanged. Three of the six endpoints never reached their handler: `/order-status-change` answered **500** (the payload used `status`; the handler reads `delivered`), `/order-assign` **302** (its validation wants `delivery_boy`, not `deliveryboy_id`), and `/update-final-price` **403** (it needs `Manage Order Refund Request`). So for half the endpoints it was asserting that a request which never ran changed nothing. Every row now carries a **reach marker** and a case whose marker fails is a failure, not a quiet pass. **Two further things that cost a diagnosis each.** `/order-assign` then answered 500 for a real reason: it deletes from **`delivery_earnings`, a GATE F table with no migration**, so it does not exist in the test database while existing in production with live data. The fixture creates it from `GATE-F-VERIFIED-SCHEMA.md:161`'s measured DDL, **guarded by `hasTable`** -- the F58 lesson, where an unguarded `Schema::create` standing in for absent schema collided with the migration that later supplied it and took ten tests down. And the 500 on an unrecognised status turned out to be a defect in its own right across three endpoints: **F81**. | Nothing outstanding on this row. E12 (P2-04-15), E13 (P2-04-16) and E14 (P2-04-17) remain, and E14 is where `StoreCurrencyConversionService`'s post-creation price rewrite belongs |
| P2-04-15 | WS E12 — **RULE: only authorised delivery and internal note changes** <br><br>**Acceptance:** delivery assignment and order notes are writable only by a principal holding the matching permission and owning the store | AUDIT-2026-10-25; `OrderNotePolicy` exists; the delivery-assignment path is unasserted | **DONE [2026-10-26]** | `tests/Feature/Security/OrderAssignPermissionTest.php` (3 tests, new); the rest was already covered by `OrderNoteAuthorizationTest` (9), `OrderMutationTest` and `tests/Feature/Tenancy/DeliveryBoyScopeTest.php` | **MOST OF THIS ROW WAS ALREADY TRUE AND UNRECORDED; ONE HALF OF IT WAS NOT TESTED AT ALL.** Gap analysis rather than a failure found it. **ORDER NOTES: both halves already existed.** `OrderNoteAuthorizationTest` drives nine cases across the API and the web: cross-tenant create refused, cross-tenant delete refused, the merchant's own store works, the platform admin may write anywhere, an unknown note type is refused, and -- the half E12 asks for -- `test_web_note_create_still_requires_the_permission`. The handler is hardened to match: `OrderNoteController` delegates to `App\Policies\OrderNotePolicy` (which checks the permission, handles the operator, and **fails closed when no tenant resolves**) and writes `$note->store_id = $order->store_id` -- **the ORDER's store, not `getCurrentStore()`**. **DELIVERY ASSIGNMENT: the tenancy half existed, the PERMISSION half did not.** `OrderMutationTest::test_merchant_cannot_assign_another_stores_order` covers a foreign order, `DeliveryBoyScopeTest` covers a foreign driver and the positive case -- and **every one of them grants `Manage Order`** and varies only the tenancy. So nothing asserted the permission was required at all: `OrderPolicy::assign()` delegating to `allow($actor, $order, 'Manage Order')` was a reading of the code, and changing that one method to `return true` would have been caught by no test. Three cases now close it: a merchant whose store, order and driver are all their own and who lacks only the permission is **refused 403 with nothing written**; the *same* principal succeeds once it is granted (without which a refusal for any reason would pass); and the platform operator succeeds **without** holding it. **THE THIRD CASE IS NOT PADDING.** `OrderPolicy::allow()` checks the platform admin FIRST, and its own comment records why: `isAbleTo()` running ahead of it locked the operator out of assign entirely, because on a database built from source no role holds `Manage Order`. A permission test that happened to use an operator would measure that bypass instead of the permission, so the two are asserted separately. **Bitten three ways, each failing exactly its own case:** `assign()` returning `true` fails only the unpermitted case; **inverting the admin ordering** -- the exact regression the comment warns about -- fails only the operator case; `assign()` returning `false` fails the positive and operator cases. | The `delivery_earnings` GATE F table has no migration, so the handler's own write to it cannot complete in the test database. The authorization is asserted unconditionally and only the assignment's completion is read behind `Schema::hasTable` -- the pattern `OrderMutationTest` established, because skipping the case would drop a guarantee unrelated to the gap |
| P2-04-16 | WS E13 — **RULE: corrections through cancellation and linked replacement orders** <br><br>**Acceptance:** the UI offers no in-place product correction; it offers cancel-and-replace, and the replacement is linked to the original | AUDIT-2026-10-25; **may require a schema field for the link — escalate before building** | **DESIGNED AND SCHEMA-REVIEWED; IMPLEMENTATION BLOCKED ON ONE COMMERCIAL QUESTION [2026-10-26]** | no code change; `docs/development/phase-2/E13-CANCEL-AND-REPLACEMENT-DESIGN.md` (the design, 253 lines), `tests/Feature/Security/OrderLineImmutabilityTest.php` (the verified half) | **THE ACCEPTANCE HAS TWO CLAUSES AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS ABOUT CODE THAT EXISTS.** **"The UI offers no in-place product correction" IS SATISFIED AND MEASURED.** E11's test posts `product_json`, `product_id`, `product_price`, `qty`, `quantity` and a `products` array at all six order-mutating endpoints and reads the row back unchanged, and a ratchet forbids mass-assigning an order from the request. That is a stronger statement than "the UI omits the control", which is what the clause asks for. **"IT OFFERS CANCEL-AND-REPLACE, AND THE REPLACEMENT IS LINKED TO THE ORIGINAL" CANNOT BE VERIFIED, because none of it exists.** Measured: `orders` has 38 columns and **no parent, replacement, original or linked-order column** -- only `return_price`, `return_status`, `return_date` and `cancel_date`. A grep across `app/`, `routes/` and the order views for replacement, reorder or duplicate-order workflows finds **nothing** (the only hits are the unrelated PreOrder module). Cancellation sets `cancel_date` and `delivered_status = 2` and stops there. So there is no replacement order to link, and no link to assert. | **DECISION REQUESTED. Building this is a new commercial workflow, and it is Phase 04's by the approved plan.** It needs: a schema change (a linked-order column, with the GATE F question of whether it already exists in production), a new order-creation path that copies a cancelled order's lines, and a rule for what happens to the original's payment and stock. CLAUDE.md puts **returns** in Phase 04 Commerce ("products, multi-warehouse stock, pricing, fulfilment, COD, **returns**, channels"), and the owner's standing limits reserve changes to order behaviour. Three ways forward: **(1)** record E13 as satisfied by its first clause and move the second to Phase 04, which is what the plan's own phase boundary suggests; **(2)** build it in Phase 02 as an explicit scope addition, with the schema and behaviour decisions taken first; **(3)** build only the LINK -- a column plus the UI showing it -- against replacements created by hand, which is half a feature and would need the same schema decision anyway. I would choose (1) and have not acted on any of them **[2026-10-26] THE OWNER'S FOUR-STEP SEQUENCE IS NOW COMPLETE FOR STEPS 1-3** — design, relations and production-schema review, and the payment and stock impact — in `E13-CANCEL-AND-REPLACEMENT-DESIGN.md`. **The decisive measurement: cancellation reverses the money but does NOT restore stock, while `return` does.** The cancel arm (`Order.php:579-598`) sets `payment_status = 'Unpaid'`, which `OrderObserver::updated()` turns into a wallet reversal through `OrderFinancialService::syncFromOrder()` — and that is **state-guarded** (paid→unpaid or unpaid→paid only), so re-asserting the same status moves no money. But no site keyed on `delivered_status = 2` increments stock; a census found the only restore call sites are the **return** path (`Order.php:1004`, `:1035`) and the payment/order rollbacks. So a replacement that takes stock again **duplicates the movement**, which is exactly what the owner forbade. **The engine already owns every primitive needed** — atomic restore (`ProductStockService::restoreProductStock/restoreVariantStock`), a per-line idempotency marker stamped into `product_json` (`Order::LINE_STOCK_RESTORED`), and a **database-level** `UNIQUE (order_id)` on `store_order_financials` barring a duplicate financial row — so "reuse the existing engine" is satisfiable. **What does not exist is the link:** no `parent_order_id`/`replacement_order_id` column in the model, in any migration, or in the verified production schema, and none of the 33 `$fillable` keys is one. So E13 needs **one additive migration**, dev/test only under D-07. **A schema hazard that must not be skipped when the tests are written:** `store_order_financials` is a **GATE F** table — it exists in production with live data and no migration creates it — so its `UNIQUE (order_id)` guard is **absent from CI, staging and the SQLite test database**. A test asserting "a duplicate financial row is impossible" proves nothing unless it creates that constraint first. **THE ONE BLOCKING QUESTION (Q1): may a replacement differ from the original at all?** If the answer is "identical lines only" — a reissue — the recommended design (S1: the replacement carries the original's stock via the existing marker, so net movement is `-N` not `-2N`) is complete and implementable now, and Q2 (a partially-paid original, which today's cancel arm reverses in full) and Q3 (the original's prices or today's) both fall away. If a replacement may change lines, stock deltas must move and **which** delta moves is a pricing-and-stock rule the standing limits reserve. Documented rather than guessed, per the owner's own instruction for this case. |
| P2-04-17 | WS E14 — **RULE: no duplication of payments or inventory movements** <br><br>**Acceptance:** no path produces a second payment row or a second stock movement for one event; cancellation and replacement included | AUDIT-2026-10-25; touches `OrderObserver` and `OrderFinancialService`; **money path — escalate any behaviour change** | **REVIEWED ACROSS 26 GATEWAY CONTROLLERS + `PaymentController`; ONE DEFECT FIXED, FIVE DECISIONS RAISED [2026-10-26]** | no code change; existing cover is `tests/Feature/Commerce/RefundStockIdempotencyTest.php` (11 tests), `PaymeraCallbackIdempotencyTest`, `RefundStateMachineTest`, `CodStateMachineTest`, and `Order::LINE_STOCK_RESTORED` | **THE STOCK HALF IS DONE, and it was done before this row existed.** `RefundStockIdempotencyTest` has eleven cases: the first restock gives the stock back, a replayed restock does not add it twice, repeated replays cannot inflate it, the restock is recorded as a **durable marker**, a second refund on the same order can still be restocked, stock returns to the variant the order records, an ambiguous variant is skipped rather than guessed, another store's variant is not restocked, and a negative refund quantity cannot subtract stock. The mechanism is `Order::LINE_STOCK_RESTORED`, a PER-LINE marker, and P1-INV-15 (E) records why per-line rather than per-order: **two endpoints could credit a returned line's stock and neither knew about the other** -- `Order::product_return()` marks one line, `OrderController::restoreReturnedStock()` loops every line -- so the two-step merchant workflow credited that line twice. Each had its own replay guard, each correct about itself. **THE PAYMENT HALF IS PARTIAL.** `PaymeraCallbackIdempotencyTest` covers one gateway's callback, and the refund and COD state machines cover their own transitions. What is NOT established is the same property across the other gateways' callbacks -- and there are nine of them, which F46's census already found to be byte-identical copies of one block, so the shape of the work is a census and one ratchet rather than nine tests. **THE "AND REPLACEMENT" CLAUSE IS NOT APPLICABLE:** no replacement workflow exists (see P2-04-16), so cancel-then-replace cannot be driven. | **What would close it:** a census of the nine gateway callbacks for a replay guard, plus one ratchet asserting every callback that writes an order checks for an existing row first -- the F46/F50 shape, where fixing the file somebody had read left the control bypassable by choosing another payment method. Not started, and deliberately not folded into this row: it is money-path work that deserves its own slice and its own evidence. `store_wallet_transactions` is a GATE F table, so any test of the wallet side needs the same guarded fixture E11's does **[2026-10-26] THE OWNER-AUTHORISED INDEPENDENT REVIEW IS DONE FOR PARTS 1-3** — `docs/development/phase-2/E14-PAYMENT-CALLBACK-REVIEW.md`. **Two corrections to the premise, both measured. (1) There are 44 configured payment methods, not nine** (`Setting::paymentList()` builds `$Setting_array[0..43]`; two are notification relays, not gateways). **(2) Most "gateway" controllers never touch a customer order** — of 26 censused, **19 write only `PlanOrder`** (SaaS subscriptions, Phase 06) and **seven** contain a live customer-`Order` writer. Counting this correctly matters: an unbounded grep for `Order::create(` also matches `PlanOrder::create(`, which inflated a first count, and that is how a census reads as complete when it is not. **THE CROSS-CUTTING RESULT: the money axis is defended, the STOCK axis is not.** Replay resistance is largely **present** — every live writer holds a `claimGatewayReturn()` claim (`Cache::add()` insert-if-absent on the checkout nonce), so the owner's first question answers *yes*, and the grep-level hypothesis "no idempotency guards anywhere" was **wrong for every one of them**. The money axis holds because Phase 01 inverted `resolveOrderPaymentStatus()`'s default to **`Pending`** with no verification record, so no wallet is credited on the weak paths. But four handlers (Khalti, PaiementPro, CinetPay, Easebuzz) decrement stock with an inline read-modify-write, **before or without any payment evidence**, and never give it back on any exit, on anonymous unthrottled routes — **denial of inventory, not free paid goods** (F90, D-13). **ONE DEFECT DRIVEN AND FIXED (F89)** because it needed no decision — a crash, not a rule: a charged card ended in HTTP 500 for any order containing a variant product, after the capture and after the order committed. Reproduced, fixed, revert-checked, with two limits recorded rather than glossed (only the accessor is proven; the siblings are **not** affected and a bare grep says otherwise). **FIVE DECISIONS RAISED, none acted on:** F87/F88 (a provider classified "verified" gates the API envelope, not the payment — and the posture test checks that a marker *exists*, not that it is *adequate*) → **D-12**; F90/F91/F92 (stock without payment evidence; a caller-supplied `store_id` selecting **which merchant's gateway credentials sign a charge**, bounded only by a hardcoded sandbox host; AuthorizeNet's capture never reconciled against the order total) → **D-13**; F95 (the two anonymous routes that write an order are unthrottled while the route above them, which writes nothing, is throttled) and F96 (the Paymera lock's 10-second TTL is held across SMTP and SMS, reopening the window it exists to close — established by the project's own refusal to hold *database* locks across those same calls). **ON THE OWNER'S CONDITION** *"لا تفترض أن Ratchet يقرأ الكود فقط يكفي"*: F89 was driven. F96 **cannot** be driven here and that is stated rather than papered over — `Cache::lock` on the `array` driver is process-local, so proving it needs MySQL plus `CACHE_STORE=database`, the `tests/Support/Security/bin/verify-*-on-mysql.php` pattern. **A trap recorded for part 4:** four handlers mint their reference as `$orderID = time()` (one-second resolution), so two requests fired back-to-back collide on a unique index and the swallowed exception **reads as idempotency**. A replay test must space its requests or it manufactures a false green. **Part 3b remains:** `PaymentService`, `Api\ApiController`, `Api\DashboardController`, `OrderController`. |

**THAT NOTE IS OBSOLETE, and is replaced here rather than deleted because the change is the single
most important fact in this file.** It read: *"B1 is the one that matters for acceptance:
everything claimed in this phase so far is verified by a contrast checker, a real browser, `php -l`
and `node --check` — never by the PHPUnit suites, which have not run here at all."*

They run now. Unit, Feature, Contract and the known-outcome gate all execute, the application boots
and serves, and the claims in this phase are measured against it rather than against a renderer.
Running it found **eight defects no static check could see** — listed in `PHASE-02-HANDOFF.md` §4,
two of them already shipped and invisible because nobody had rendered the one configuration that
exposed them.

## Findings recorded, not acted on

| # | Finding | Why it is only recorded |
|---|---|---|
| F0 | **`orders.delivered_status` is one integer column carrying four different concepts.** 0–6 are fulfilment states, **7 is Partially Paid (a payment state)** and **8 is Pre Order (an order type)**. The column's own migration comment still reads `0=>pending/1=>diliver/2=>cancel/3=>return` and has been stale for a long time. | Phase 02 surfaces this honestly rather than hiding it — the dashboard now labels 7 and 8 as "recorded in the same field, but not fulfilment states" — but **splitting the column is a schema and commerce change, which is Phase 04 and an owner decision**. Recorded here so the next phase inherits the fact rather than rediscovering it. |
| F1 | `HomeController::calculatePercentageToday()` computes today against the **all-time total**, not a day-over-day change, and all nine `*Per` variables it feeds are **unused by every view in the repository**. | The dashboard never displayed them, so this is dead computation rather than a visible defect. Removing it also removes 7 `COUNT` queries per dashboard load — worth doing, but it is a controller change with no user-visible effect and belongs in its own commit. |
| F2 | `resources/lang/ar.json` contained **17 duplicated keys** (2,874 entries, 2,857 distinct); `en.json` had none. | **RESOLVED, AND NOT ON PURPOSE — which is the part worth recording.** This row said de-duplicating should be an owner decision because it rewrites a translation file nobody asked to have rewritten. Several Phase 02 increments then added keys by decoding and re-encoding the file, and a round-trip through an associative array silently drops duplicates. So the rewrite happened as a side effect of adding a string. **MEASURED against `main` before stating it was harmless:** 17 duplicated keys on `main`, **0 effective values changed, 0 keys lost** — a re-encode keeps the same occurrence `json_decode` already kept, so the 17 dead lines went and every effective string is identical. Both files now have 0 duplicates. Pinned going forward by `tests/Unit/Localisation/TranslationFileIntegrityTest` (5 cases: valid JSON, no duplicate keys in either file, and no Arabic-only orphan beyond the 31 inherited) — bite-tested by reintroducing a duplicate and an orphan, which fails 2. |
| F3 | Host-chrome controls outside `.ss-scope` (e.g. the layout's breadcrumb links) have **no focus ring**. | The design system never styles a bare element selector, so an opt-in stylesheet cannot fix it. It is a host-chrome change, not a Phase 02 one. |
| **F4** | **RESOLVED — the customised sidebar is gated. The row's own `module`/`permission` keys were worse than unchecked: `side_menus_option` HAS NEITHER COLUMN (migration, production dump, and both seeders, which carry the values in PHP and drop them on insert), so there was nothing to check. The gate is recovered from the LISTENERS, which are the authoritative `name => [module, permission]` map, via `Menu::$denied`; the operator limb is first, so the principal who configures this sidebar loses nothing. Module entitlement additionally comes from `Route::has()` — an add-on registers routes only while `add_on_managers` holds its row — which also fixes an availability defect, since the old `route($name)` THREW from inside the sidebar for an uninstalled module. 22 tests across two files; deleting the production call site fails one of them** <br><br> *The original write-up follows, kept because the reasoning is the record:* **`generateSideMenu()`'s database-sourced rows bypass BOTH `Menu::add()` gates.** `getSideMenu()` (`helper.php:2170-2188`) pushes `SideMenuOption` rows straight onto `$menu->menu`, carrying `module` and `permission` keys that **nothing ever checks** — `Menu::add()` is not called, and `generateSideMenu()` (`:2193-2284`) contains no `isAbleTo` and no `ActivatedModule`. The platform-level rows (`created_by = 0`, `store_id = null`, selected at `:2147`) therefore render for **every merchant**. | Found by the review of P2-01-10. Only reachable when the `SidebarCustomization` module is active. **Pre-existing, in a function Phase 02 did not change, and a real authorization hole** — it wants its own change with a test, not a line in a refactor commit. |
| **F5** | **RESOLVED — database-sourced output is escaped. `title` through `e()`, `icon` allowlisted to `[A-Za-z0-9-]` because it sits inside a class attribute where a quote alone breaks out, and an external row's href scheme allowlisted to http/https because escaping leaves `javascript:` intact and clickable. The `window_type = iframe` branch used to run AFTER the resolvers and OVERWRITE them, undoing both controls; it now honours the drop first and checks the embedded url too** <br><br> *The original write-up follows, kept because the reasoning is the record:* **`generateSideMenu()` renders database-sourced titles unescaped** (`helper.php:2267`, `__($item['title']) . '</span>'`). Combined with F4's editable, platform-level rows this is a stored-XSS shape. | Same function as F4 and the same reasoning: `generateMenu()` (which Phase 02 converted) only ever receives hardcoded listener titles, so the escaping added there is hardening. This path needs the same treatment. |
| **F6** | **The dashboard order chart built platform data for merchant staff — latently.** `getOrderChart()` branched on `type == 'admin'`, but a staff user's `type` is **their role name** (`UserController::store`: `$user->type = $role_r->name`). Every staff member therefore took the else-branch and got `PlanOrder` counts — platform-wide SaaS subscription orders with **no `store_id` predicate at all** (`PlanOrder` is not in `scoped_models` and registers no `StoreScope`). **LATENT, not live:** `$chartData` is rendered only by `superadmin/dashboard.blade.php` and `theme_analytic.blade.php`; the merchant dashboard references it **0 times**, so the counts were computed and discarded. My first write-up said staff "were shown" it — an overclaim, caught in review and corrected. | **FIXED in P2-02-12.** Fixed now rather than later because this inventory marks the chart "Reuse" — Phase 02 intends to render it, which is when latent becomes live. The discriminator now asks `UserPolicy::isOperator()`. The reviewer also established the fix is **monotone**: the new PlanOrder set (`isOperator`) is a strict subset of the old (`type !== 'admin'`), so no principal can newly receive that data. |
| **F7** | **Menu titles are translated twice.** `CompanyMenuListener` passes `'title' => __('Dashboard')` (already translated) and `menuTree()` — like the string builder before it — calls `__()` on that result again. It works only because the second lookup misses and returns its input. | Harmless today and pre-existing on both sides of the refactor. Fixing it means deciding which layer owns translation across every module listener — wider than this phase. |
| **F8** | **RESOLVED — dark mode is AA. MEASURED IN THE RUNNING APPLICATION across LTR/RTL x light/dark: 14 of 32 readings were below AA, now 0 of 32. `#9DA3B4` is the same hue lifted — 5.66:1 on the sidebar/table surface and 6.14:1 on the breadcrumb bar, against 3.71 and 4.03. In `custom.css`/`rtl-custom.css` rather than `style-dark.css`, because that file is a host theme asset an update would revert, and scoped to dark through a `data-ss-theme` hook the layout now publishes on EVERY page — it was in a partial two views include, so every other screen had no hook at all. Reverting both stylesheets returns exactly 14 failures** <br><br> *The original write-up follows, kept because the reasoning is the record:* **Sidebar navigation was illegible on every phone and tablet, on default settings — and two false readings stood between the screenshot and that sentence.** `transprent-bg` (which the default `cust_theme_bg = 'on'` puts on the nav) is acted on only inside `@media (min-width: 1025px)` in `assets/css/style.css`. At 1024px and below the nav falls back to `.dash-sidebar { background: #1c232f }` while `custom.css` keeps `color: #333333`. Measured in Chromium at 360/390/768/1024px, en and ar: **label 1.25:1, icon ~1.07:1 (`#060606`), sidebar-search input text ~1.30:1 (`#293240`)**. The same page at 1440px measures **12.01:1**, which is why desktop screenshots and a desktop probe both called it clean. **False reading 1:** a probe re-pointed at `file://` returned `rgb(0,0,238)` on white and passed all six variants at 9.40:1 — browser-default link blue on a bare page, because `file://` resolves `/assets/css/…` at the filesystem root and **no stylesheet loaded**. **False reading 2:** my preview hard-coded sidebar.blade.php's `else` branch, so the first 1.25:1 I saw was a non-default configuration and I nearly filed it as this slice's defect. | **FIXED** for light mode below 1025px, in `public/css/custom.css` and `public/css/rtl-custom.css`: below the breakpoint the sidebar *is* dark by the host's own design, so the labels, icons, arrows and search field are made light rather than the background being changed. Scoped `@media (max-width: 1024px)` at specificity (0,3,0) — beats the (0,2,0) base rule, **loses** to `.active`/`:hover`/`:focus` at (0,4,0), which keep their `#fff` (verified: active stays `rgb(255,255,255)` at 390px and 1440px). No `!important`, so `style-dark.css`'s `color: #808191 !important` still wins and **dark mode is deliberately unchanged**. `#808191`, the host's own dark-sidebar grey, was **not** reused: on `#1c232f` it reaches only 4.11:1, under the 4.5:1 that 15px/500 needs. Guarded by 10 new checks in `browser-checks-p02.cjs` across 5 widths x 2 languages; **bite-tested** — reverting the two CSS files returns exactly 8 failures and the 1440px pair still passes. **Still open, tracked here:** dark mode is 4.03:1 (`on`) and 3.71:1 (`off`), and `cust_theme_bg = 'off'` in light is still 1.25:1 above 1025px (screenshot `07-…-custthemebg-off.png`). Those need `public/assets/css/style-dark.css`, where the value is `!important` on a selector list **shared with `p` and `.dataTable-info`** — admin-wide across 70 modules, and **with Composer blocked the Feature suite cannot run**, so that change has no regression evidence available. Own slice. |
| **F9** | **RESOLVED — the selected item is AA on every preset, with NO brand colour changed. The background stays exactly what the merchant chose; only the LABEL flips to whichever of white or near-black is readable on it, computed from the brand's own WCAG luminance. Across the ten presets white wins on six and near-black on four, and every one reaches AA — worst case 4.90:1 where six were below 4.5:1. A picker colour goes through the same arithmetic, so it cannot be a theme nobody checked. `--ss-brand-ontext` does the same for the brand used as LINK TEXT on a light surface (the breadcrumb, 2.48:1), darkening only until AA is met — six of ten presets come out unchanged. **No new global brand-colour policy was needed, so nothing was escalated**** <br><br> *The original write-up follows, kept because the reasoning is the record:* **The selected navigation item fails AA at every width, desktop included.** `.dash-item.active > .dash-link` is `#fff` on `var(--theme-color)`; on theme-3's `#10B77F` that measures **2.59:1**. So the merchant's current location is the least legible label on the screen, and `@media (min-width: 1025px)` has nothing to do with it. | Not fixed, and not fixable from this slice. `--theme-color` is the **merchant's own brand colour** — ten preset themes plus a custom colour picker (`app.blade.php:25-40`) — so there is no single safe value: a correct fix either picks the label colour from the chosen colour's computed luminance or darkens the active background, for all ten presets and any custom value. That is a theming decision with a visible brand consequence, which makes it an owner call rather than a drive-by in a navigation refactor. The new browser checks deliberately measure the **default** state only, and say so in the code, so their pass cannot be read as "the sidebar is accessible". |
| **F10** | **Two operator checks in one file now disagree about who the operator is.** `HomeController::index():139` dispatches to the Super Admin dashboard on the exact literal `$user->type == 'super admin'`, while `getOrderChart():530` now normalises through `UserPolicy::isOperator()` (`strtolower(rtrim(...))`). For a hand-edited `'Super Admin'` row the two answer differently: `index()` renders the merchant dashboard while `getOrderChart` builds `PlanOrder` counts. | Found by the second independent review, which judged it **a ledger line rather than a code change**, and that is accepted. It is doubly latent — the merchant view discards `$chartData` (F6), and the role path cannot produce the casing because `RolePolicy::RESERVED_NAMES` blocks `admin` and every `super admin` spelling, so it takes a hand-edited row. But it is one more instance of the pattern P1-SEC-11 exists to end, in a file `OperatorCheckCensusTest`'s construct assertion does **not** cover (that names only `OrderPolicy`, `OrderDocumentAccessGuard` and `OrderController`). Changing `index()`'s dispatch changes **which dashboard a principal is shown**, so it is a behaviour change needing its own test, not a drive-by in a chart fix. |
| **F11** | **A merchant whose `users.type` is `admin` now has TWO store switchers on every page.** `partision/header.blade.php:130-184` has shipped one all along — current store at `:141`, `route('change.store', …)` at `:151` — gated to `type == 'admin'` and listing `auth()->user()->stores`, the `hasMany(Store,'created_by','id')` relation. P2-01-11 added the sidebar chip, which serves **staff and the operator too** (the relation returns nothing for staff, so they have never had a switcher, nor any indication of which store they were in). An earlier version of the chip's own docblock claimed nothing in the repository linked to `change.store`; that was wrong, found by the independent review, and corrected in place — it had been the justification on record for the component. | **Owner decision, not a defect.** Removing the header switcher takes away a control admins use today, and the chip is not an unconditional replacement: a null `current_store` leaves it out entirely, which would leave that admin with none. The two are kept until the owner decides. Note the chip is the broader of the two in *who sees it*, so "should staff switch stores at all?" is a product question that comes with it — `StoreScope::admissibleStoreIds()` already admits the employer's stores and `changeStore()` already accepted staff switching, so this surfaces an existing capability rather than widening one. |
| **F12** | **RESOLVED — `/change-store/{id}` is a POST. The route NAME is unchanged and both callers became posting forms with a token; the workspace chip keeps `role="option"`, `aria-selected` and `.ss-menu__item` on the submit control with `role="none"` on the interposed form, so the three browser checks reading those selectors measure the same thing. A GET now answers 405 having moved nothing. CSRF enforcement is asserted STRUCTURALLY, because `ValidateCsrfToken` short-circuits in every PHPUnit run — asserting a 419 would have been a red test proving nothing** <br><br> *The original write-up follows, kept because the reasoning is the record:* **`change.store` is a `GET` that mutates the tenant pointer**, with no CSRF token (`routes/web.php:841`). Reachable from a third-party `<img>` or link, and by link prefetching. | **Pre-existing** — `header.blade.php:151` has shipped that link since before Phase 02 — and `changeStore()` still ownership-checks the target, so the worst case is a merchant's own active store being flipped to another of their own. Recorded now because P2-01-11 makes switching a first-class, always-visible feature rather than an admin-only menu item, which raises the odds of it being exercised. Making it a POST is a route and a form, plus every caller. |
| **F13** | **`generateSideMenu()` is never zoned and never will be.** With `SidebarCustomization` active and `side_menus_option` rows present, `getSideMenu()` ends in `generateSideMenu()` (`helper.php:2290`), a separate string builder that P2-01-10 deliberately did not convert. Those tenants get a flat sidebar with no zone headings. | Not a defect — the kill switch produces the same thing — but worth knowing before anyone reads "zones" as universal. Zoning that path means converting it first, which is the same change F4 and F5 need, with the same regression evidence. |

### Findings opened by this cycle

| # | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| **F14** | **`store/change-status` is a state-changing GET** writing `stores.is_active` from query input, one screen up the same controller from F12 — so an `<img src>` on any page a signed-in merchant visits takes their storefront offline, no token and no click, and link prefetching reaches it too. `resolveOwnedStore()` confines it to the caller's own stores and the last-active-store guard saves a single-store merchant, so a merchant with two or more stores is the exposed case. **The sweep that produced F12 missed it**, which is the more useful half of this row: that sweep was keyed on the one route somebody was looking at. | **FIXED.** POST, with both jQuery callers sending the token and the three existing security drives re-pointed. |
| **F15** | **`isAbleTo('Manage Product')` — SINGULAR — exists nowhere**: not in `Utility`'s seeded set, not in the live permissions table, not in any module seeder. Laratrust answers false for a name nothing holds, so the dashboard's out-of-stock link, top-products list and product onboarding step were hidden from **every** principal including the tenant owner, silently and with no error anywhere. | **FIXED** (P2-02-13), with a ratchet that reads grantable names from the seeders. |
| **F16** | **`asset('public/...')` appears ~100 times across 32 view files.** `asset()` already serves from `public/`, so each one resolves to `/public/...` and 404s. The admin layout's RTL-dark stylesheet was one of them, which is why **Arabic + dark mode loaded no layout CSS at all**. | **The admin layout is FIXED and pinned** by `AdminLayoutAssetsTest`, which resolves every `asset()` path in it against `public/`. **[2026-10-26] `partision/footerlink.blade.php` IS NOW FIXED TOO, and it was the worst of them.** The layout INCLUDES that partial, so its three broken paths were live on **every admin page**: `dropzone.min.js`, `select2.min.js` and `html2pdf.bundle.min.js` all 404d (measured: `/public/assets/js/plugins/dropzone.min.js` 404 vs `/assets/js/plugins/dropzone.min.js` 200). The first two are corrected; the third is **removed rather than corrected** — see F64. Correcting it alone armed `Dropzone.autoDiscover` globally, which is F63. The consequences were not cosmetic — `Dropzone is not defined` and `$(...).select2 is not a function` on load, so the product editor's image dropzone and its tag/category pickers did nothing, and `#submit-all`'s handler (whose first statements reach for Dropzone) could not save a product at all. This is what the previous session recorded as an unexplained "environmental" blocker on P2-03-3; it was F16. **Four more ADMIN screens were found and fixed on the same day — see F65** — after which `grep` over every view that reaches `layouts.app` shows **zero** live `asset('public/...')` left. **The remaining ~26 files are still NOT fixed** — payment pages, theme layouts, the marketplace, `front_end`: a different blast radius with different callers to verify. Recorded for a separate sweep. |
| **F17** | **`SideMenuController::update()` has no ownership predicate and its permission check is commented out** (vendored, `packages/workdo/SidebarCustomization`). It does `SideMenuOption::find($id)` then writes `name`/`title` from request input, so **any merchant can rewrite any sidebar row by id** — including a `created_by = 0` PLATFORM row, which the write then re-homes to themselves and thereby deletes from every other tenant's sidebar. | **NOT FIXED — vendored code, and out of this phase's scope.** It is also what makes F5's escaping load-bearing rather than hardening: that escaping is now the thing standing between this and stored XSS in every merchant's admin chrome. Raised by the independent review. Needs its own host-side guard, like `OrderDocumentAccessGuard`. |
| **F18** | **The gate recovered in F4 covers *unentitled*, not *uninstalled* or *setting-disabled*.** `Menu::$denied` can only refuse a name a listener OFFERED, and three conditional blocks in `CompanyMenuListener` (tax, WooCommerce, Shopify) skip whole groups when a setting or plan flag is off, while the seeded rows persist. A module with no `add_on_managers` row registers no listener at all. | **STATED, not fixed.** The route-existence check covers the module half wherever the row names a module route; it does not cover first-party routes that are always registered. Closing it properly means denylisting from the seeders' own arrays rather than from runtime listener calls. |
| **F19** | **The order list's status badges rendered `Jan 1, 1970`** for orders whose status date is null — an epoch date presented as a real one. Seen in the live screenshots. | **FIXED** in P2-04-4a. Root cause: `Utility::dateFormat()` is `date($fmt, strtotime($date))` and `strtotime(null)` is FALSE, so `date($fmt, false)` is the epoch. Fixed in `OrderDataTable` only — see F20 for why not in the helper. |
| **F20** | **`Utility::dateFormat(null)` renders the Unix epoch, at 64 call sites.** It is `date($settings, strtotime($date))`; `strtotime(null)` and `strtotime('')` both return FALSE and `date($fmt, false)` is `date($fmt, 0)`. So ANY screen passing a nullable date column to this helper can print a date the row does not have. | **NOT FIXED, deliberately.** 28 of the 64 call sites are inside vendored `packages/workdo/` modules, so changing the helper alters output across seventy modules with **no regression evidence available for any of them** — a sweep with an owner decision behind it, not a line in a list fix. F19's surface is fixed locally and this row is the sweep's entry point. The safe shape is the one `OrderDataTable::hasDate()` now uses. |
| **F21** | **`Order::getReturnDateAttribute()` carries the same epoch, and `$appends` puts it on EVERY order serialisation** — including the Flutter API's. `date('Y-m-d', strtotime($this->order_date))` with a null `order_date` returns `"1970-01-01"`. Found because a test asserting across the whole DataTables payload failed on it. | **NOT FIXED — a public-response change.** `return_date` going from `"1970-01-01"` to null is visible to the shipped app on a path it reads, which the brief puts behind an owner decision. The case also needs an `order_date` that no production create path leaves null, so it is latent rather than live. |
| **F22** | **Dates render with ENGLISH month names inside the Arabic admin.** The live Arabic order list shows `Oct 7, 2026` beside fully translated headers and labels. `Utility::dateFormat()` uses PHP's `date()`, which has no locale, rather than `isoFormat()`/`translatedFormat()`. | **NOT FIXED.** Same helper and same 64 call sites as F20, so it is the same sweep and the same owner decision; the fix is one line inside `dateFormat()` once that sweep is authorised. Recorded now so the Arabic screenshots are not read as showing correct output. |
| **F23** | **An operator's "Mark all as read" was a PLATFORM-WIDE WRITE.** `MerchantNotificationController::scope()` omits the `store_id` predicate for an operator — right for the three reads, because reading across stores is their contract — so `markAllRead`'s mass `update()` carried only `type IN (…) AND is_read = 0`. One POST by a `super admin` flipped `is_read` on **every tenant's** rows, clearing every merchant's badge, with no mark-unread anywhere to undo it. **My own comment on that line claimed the opposite of what the code did.** | **FIXED before the slice shipped.** The write is now always confined to one store, taken from `$user->current_store` **directly** — not `getCurrentStore()`, which answers the literal **1** for a `super admin` with no current store and would have silently mass-marked whichever tenant is store 1. An operator who is not looking at a store marks nothing. Two tests, both bite. Raised by the independent review. |
| **F24** | **The notification bell rendered only for `users.type == 'admin'`**, because the `<li>` was placed inside the header's owner-only conditional. Staff `users.type` is their ROLE NAME (`UserController::store`), never the literal `'admin'` — so **a staff member holding `Manage Order`, the exact principal the per-type authorisation was built to serve, had no link to `/notifications` anywhere in the application**, and neither did the operator. It also made the comment justifying the no-permission-check decision describe a state that could not occur. | **FIXED.** The bell moved outside that block. Pinned by a test that drives a real staff member over HTTP and reads the rendered header, rather than grepping the Blade source — a source grep would pass with the `<li>` inside any conditional at all. Bites. Raised by the independent review. |
| **F25** | **`test_a_principal_with_no_current_store_is_served_nothing` could not fail.** `where('store_id', null)` is rewritten by Laravel to `store_id IS NULL`, and `store_notifications.store_id` is NOT NULL, so no row matches whether or not the guard is there — the row assertion was free. | **FIXED** by counting queries instead: 0 against `store_notifications` with the guard, 2 without. A companion case asserts a resolved principal DOES reach the table, so "0 queries" cannot be satisfied by a listener registered too late or a renamed table. Both bite. Raised by the independent review. |
| **F26** | **Three more weak tests in the same file.** (a) the non-integer-id case posted a HAND-WRITTEN uri and asserted 404 — which a nonexistent uri also returns; (b) its named hazard (MySQL coercing `'5abc'` to 5) **cannot be reproduced on SQLite at all**, so removing `filter_var` left it green; (c) the route-takeover premise reasoned about a module winning the uri slot, but **no WorkDo module loads in any test run**, so it was structurally blind to that case. | **FIXED/STATED:** (a) now goes through `route()`, with a premise drive proving the well-formed id is accepted on the same route — ordered AFTER the row assertion, because the premise drive is itself a write; (b) the SQLite limitation is stated in the docblock rather than implied, and the control is kept because it is needed on MySQL; (c) the docblock now says what the assertion does and does not cover. The type ratchet's regex was also widened from `[a-z_]` to `[A-Za-z0-9_]`. Raised by the independent review. |
| **F27** | **The "View order" button followed the wrong permission, and a docblock asserted the two were the same.** The notification gates on `Manage Order`; `OrderController::order_preview` gates on **`Show Order`**. A staff member holding the first and not the second was offered a button that answered 403. | **FIXED.** The view is told separately whether the caller holds `Show Order`. Not a hole — the preview fails closed, which the new test measures rather than assumes — but it had no test that would have caught it regressing, and now does. Bites. Raised by the independent review. |
| **F28** | **Two different situations gave one wrong explanation.** `context()` returns null both for an unresolved tenant and for an empty readable-type set, and the view rendered both as "your role does not include reading them" — so **a merchant whose store was deleted or deactivated was told it was a permissions problem**. No test rendered that branch at all, so a crash in it would have shipped. | **FIXED:** `noTenant` is computed separately and gets its own empty state, and a test now renders the branch and asserts it withholds the row it exists to withhold. Raised by the independent review. |
| **F29** | **The host tells the app that a Pre Order is "Pending".** `App\Models\Order`'s `order_status_text` (API response) is assigned `__('Pending')` as a default and then overwritten only for statuses 0–6 — so **7 (Partially Paid) and 8 (Pre Order) are reported to the Flutter app as Pending.** The same block also looks up `__('Comfirmed')`, a misspelling no translation file can match. | **FIXED [2026-10-26] ON THE OWNER'S WRITTEN APPROVAL**, which was explicitly for display and translation and NOT for the status rules. `Order::order_detail()` initialises `order_status_text` to `__('Pending')` and its if-chain covered 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6 — so 7 and 8 kept the default, and the shipped app renders that string. The two missing mappings are added using `HomeController::ORDER_STATUS_PARTIALLY_PAID` / `ORDER_STATUS_PRE_ORDER` and the WEB PANEL'S OWN STRINGS (`OrderDataTable:191-192` renders exactly `__('Partially Paid')` and `__('Pre Order')`), so the API now agrees with the panel rather than inventing a third vocabulary — the drift P2-04-4 found between the dashboard and the order list. Both strings were already translated (`مدفوع جزئياً`, `طلب مسبق`). **THE CONTRACT IS UNCHANGED:** `order_status` still carries the numeric 7 or 8 exactly as before, so a client that switches on the number is unaffected; only the human-readable string stops being wrong. Contract suite 26/26. **TESTED OVER ALL NINE STATUSES, not two cases** (`tests/Feature/ApiContract/OrderStatusTextTest.php`, 3 tests / 33 assertions): the defect is not "two were missed" but "the default is a real status name, so a miss is invisible" — any tenth value would fall through identically. The test enumerates the statuses from the constants, requires each to produce its own text AND its own number, and pins the constants' values as a premise. **Bitten** by removing the status-7 mapping, which reproduced `status 7 reports "Pending"` verbatim. **One ledger claim is corrected:** this row previously said the misspelled `Comfirmed` matches no translation file. It does — `en.json:1984` and `ar.json:2015` (`تأكيد`) — so the spelling is ugly but not a missing translation, and it is left alone rather than renamed, because the key is what the lang files hold. The app-side half remains fixed in P2-07-6. |
| **F30** | **Three merchant toggles were unauthorised cross-tenant writes on a GET.** `ProductBrandController::changeStatus`/`changePopular` and `ProductLabelController::changeStatus` did `find($request->id)` then wrote, with **no permission check, no ownership check and no tenant predicate** — and neither model is in `scoped_models`. Any authenticated web caller could disable or promote any brand, and disable any label, in **any store**, by walking the id. `status` is what the storefront filters on, so the effect is a competitor's brand hidden from their own shop. | **FIXED in P2-05-1**, with 15 tests and every control bite-tested. The most useful detail: **the Flutter siblings had been fixed long before** — `Api\DashboardController::ChangeStatus` and friends carry `whereIn('store_id', $this->ownedStoreIds())`. A sweep closed the API path and left the web path open, which is the fourth time a census keyed on the surface somebody was looking at has produced a defect here. |
| **F31** | **`refund/{id}/{user_id}` issues a plan refund on a GET**, and `order_approve/{id}` / `order_reject/{id}` approve or reject a bank-transfer order the same way. Reachable from a third-party `<img src>` and by link prefetching. | **NOT FIXED — money paths, and an owner decision.** Converting a verb on a refund or an order approval is a behaviour change on a money path, which this phase is explicitly not authorised to make. **Declared and classified** in `StateChangingGetInventoryTest::DECLARED` so they are visible rather than lost, and so the ratchet fails if anyone adds another. Five more subscription-workflow routes are in the same group. |
| **F32** | **~48 payment-gateway callbacks are GETs that write**, because the gateway redirects the customer's browser to them. | **NOT A DEFECT, and declared so it is not re-reported.** The verb is the gateway's to choose; changing it breaks payment capture. Recorded because a future "no state-changing GET" sweep will find them and needs the classification rather than re-deriving it. |
| **F33** | **The dev fixture was missing four permissions its own screens gate on**, so `/product-label` redirected to the dashboard and the brand toggles answered 403 for the fixture merchant — which looked exactly like a lockout introduced by P2-05-1's own tightening. The seeded customers also had no `regiester_date` (sic), which `customer/status.blade.php` requires before rendering the status switch, so the customer list showed three raw zeros where the switch belongs. | **FIXED in the fixture, not the product.** All five permissions are seeded in `app/Models/Utility.php` and granted to a real tenant owner's role there, so this brought `database/seed-p2dev.php` in line with production. Recorded because the first browser run reported six failures that were all fixture gaps, and reading them as product defects would have produced a wrong fix. |
