Two cases failed at once — the ones asserting a 200 and a write. The other three kept passing, because **a 405 satisfies "the row did not change" exactly as well as a working guard does**: defect shape #1 in this project's own list. | **FIXED — all five drives moved, not only the two that went red.** And the bite test then showed something worse: with every drive on POST, deleting the handler's ownership block STILL left the file green, because `Customer` IS in `scoped_models` and the global scope already excludes a foreign row for a principal who resolves a tenant. A case was added for a principal who resolves NONE — where `null_tenant => 'open'` means the scope contributes nothing and the handler's own `empty($currentStore)` limb is the only control. That one fails when the block is removed. |
| **F35** | **A new web route duplicated an existing DECLARED uri.** `routes/web.php` gained `Route::get('notifications', ...)` while `routes/api.php:347` already declared `Route::get('notifications', ...)` for the delivery boy. At runtime they do not collide (the api one sits inside `Route::prefix('admin')`), but `RouteShadowingTest` compares the declared strings and is right to — "only the later declaration survives registration, with no error" is how an authenticated `order-view/{id}` once lost its slot to an unauthenticated copy. | **FIXED** by renaming the web uri to `merchant-notifications`, matching `merchant-search` on this phase's other new route. The route NAMES are unchanged, so no caller moved. **Found by the project's own guard, not by review** — and only when the full Feature suite ran, which is the argument for running it at an integration milestone rather than trusting the targeted suites. |
| **F36** | **Every merchant's notification inbox is a MIXED-LANGUAGE list, and the `__()` calls around it translate nothing.** `StoreNotificationService` keys its two notification types in **different languages** — `orderCreated` uses Arabic literals (`__('طلب جديد')`), `orderPaid` uses English ones (`__('Order paid')`) — and **all four keys are missing from BOTH `en.json` and `ar.json`** (measured). `__()` returns its key when a lookup misses, so a new-order notification is **always Arabic** and a paid-order notification is **always English**, for every merchant in every locale. Seen in the P2-06-3 screenshots: the English-locale feed renders Arabic titles. | **NOT FIXED — recorded with its measurement.** Adding the four keys is the easy half and would make the language follow the request's locale. The hard half is that the text is RENDERED AND STORED AT WRITE TIME, so it is frozen in the locale of whoever triggered the order — often the SHOPPER, not the merchant reading it. Fixing that properly means storing a key plus params and rendering on read, which changes what `title` and `message` contain for the shipped Flutter app that reads them directly. Both halves change user-visible content on a public path, so this is an owner decision. The web surface renders faithfully whatever is stored; it is not the defect. |
| **F37** | **Two defects found by LOOKING AT A SCREENSHOT, both invisible to every test that existed.** (a) The notification feed ordered by `orderByDesc('id')`, which equals reverse-chronological only while ids and timestamps ascend together — and the Flutter sibling orders by `latest()`, i.e. `created_at`, so the two surfaces could disagree about which notification is newest. (b) The feed TIMESTAMP measured **4.13:1 in light mode on an UNREAD row**: it sits on `--ss-surface-sunken` there rather than the plain surface, and `--ss-fg-subtle` clears AA on one and not the other. | **BOTH FIXED.** (a) `orderByDesc('created_at')->orderByDesc('id')` — honest intent, deterministic tiebreak (the column is second-precision), and it matches the API. Pinned by a test whose fixture makes id order and time order DISAGREE on purpose, because one built the normal way cannot tell the two apart. (b) `--ss-fg-muted`, which clears both surfaces (5.94 and 5.23). **The contrast run had missed it by luck**: the fixture's newest row happened to be the READ one, so the probe sampled the lighter surface. The probe now measures the timestamp on BOTH rows, as it already did for the title and the message — 64 readings, 0 below AA. Same lesson as F8, third time. |

| **F38** | **Three icons had no glyph, and nothing anywhere said so.** `ti-layout-sidebar-right-expand` is not in the bundled `public/assets/fonts/tabler-icons.min.css`, which has `ti-layout-sidebar-right` and no `-expand` variant at all. The quick-preview button on the **order list** (P2-04-5) and on the **product list** (P2-03-6) therefore painted an empty 18px box for as long as either feature had existed, in every screenshot of both lists. `ti-category` in the storefront menu builder is the third — the font has no `ti-categor*` anything. A missing icon name fails silently and completely: the class is valid CSS, the `::before` is generated, the font loads, and the character is simply not in it. **Found by cropping one table row at 3x and counting the glyphs** — three where there should have been four. | **FIXED**, all three. Ratcheted twice, because the two questions are different: `Tests\Unit\Ui\IconNamesExistTest` reads the expectation **out of the font file itself** (no allowlist, so a font upgrade that drops a name fails here rather than going blank in production) and names the offending file; `check-row-actions-live.cjs` asserts the **painted** result, `getBoundingClientRect().width > 0` on every icon-only action, which is the thing that actually matters and which no amount of source reading can establish. Both bite. |
| **F39** | **`title` is not an accessible name, and three of the four order row actions had nothing else.** `data-bs-toggle="tooltip"` makes Bootstrap MOVE the title into `data-bs-original-title` and blank the attribute on initialisation; `aria-describedby` is attached only while the tooltip is open. So after page load, Show, Edit and Delete were unnamed controls wrapping unlabelled `<i>` elements — "button", four times per row, down the whole list. The product list's Edit and Delete were the same. **The DOM a server-side test renders has a name and the page a merchant uses does not**, which is why this had to be measured in a browser after the scripts ran. | **FIXED.** Every row action on both lists now carries an `aria-label` that names the verb **and the record** (`Delete order ORD-2047`, `Edit Damask Cotton Scarf`), because a screen-reader user moving through the actions hears them without the row's context; icons are `aria-hidden`. 7 new keys in `ar.json`/`en.json`. The live check asserts the name the way the accname spec resolves it — `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, own text, then `title` **last and from the live attribute** — so a tooltip plugin that has emptied it is reflected rather than papered over. **Bitten:** removing one `aria-label` while leaving its `title` in place returns 4 failures, which is the measurement behind this row. |
| **F40** | **`Form::open()` with no `Form::close()`** in `product/action.blade.php` — the `</form>` was never emitted. | **FIXED** (`{!! Form::close() !!}` added; the order list's equivalent already had one) **and then MEASURED, which shrank the claim.** This row first said the form's extent was "whatever the browser's error recovery allowed rather than the one button it is for". Driving the product list with and without the close tag renders an **identical DOM**: 10 forms, one `<button>` each, four descendants each, the same actions. The cell is injected as a DataTables fragment, so the parser closes the form at the end of the fragment either way. It is a **well-formedness fix with no behaviour change** — still right, because the next control added to that cell would inherit a real bug, but it did not have one, and the original wording implied a consequence that was looked for and is not there. Recorded because a bite test that comes back clean has to be explained, not quietly dropped. |
| **F41** | **A card's footer floated mid-card whenever it was the shorter of two.** `.ss-row` is a flex container and stretches its cards to the tallest one; `.ss-card` was a plain block, so a shorter card's sunken footer band stayed where its content ended and the card painted empty surface below it — measured at **40px** on the dashboard. Invisible for as long as the two cards happened to hold the same number of rows, which is why it appeared the same day P2-02-9's best-selling card first held any. | **FIXED** — `.ss-card` is a flex column with `.ss-card__body { flex: 1 1 auto }`. `1 1 auto` and not `1`: `flex: 1` resets the basis to 0 and would re-distribute every card body's height application-wide. Re-checked against everything that drives the shared stylesheet — 159/159 Phase 02 browser checks, 66/66 design-system checks, 76 contrast readings none below AA, and the live tools. **Bitten:** reverting returns 36–40px in the three stretched configurations and nothing on mobile, where the row wraps and there is no stretching to do. |
| **F42** | **Stored XSS in the order list's customer cell, shopper → merchant session.** `OrderDataTable`'s `customer_id` column printed `CustomerData->name` and `->mobile` with no escaping, and `customer_id` is in the same `rawColumns()` list the P2-04-4 status change edits — so yajra does not escape it either. Registration validates `first_name` as `'required','string','max:255'` and nothing more, so a shopper of the merchant's own store chooses the string that lands in the merchant's admin origin. **The one layer that looks like it would stop this does not:** `app/Http/Middleware/XSS.php` runs `htmlspecialchars_decode()` and then strips four strings — `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `javascript`, `alert`. The decode turns an encoded `&lt;` into a raw `<` *before* the strip looks for `&lt;`, and a raw `<` was never in the list; `<img src=x onerror=…>` passes through whole. Same-tenant escalation, not cross-tenant: `Customer` IS in `scoped_models`, so the attacker must register with the victim's own store, which is free. **Found by the `tenant-authz-reviewer` pass over the P2-04-4 change** — on the line that change edits, not in it. | **FIXED** — `e()` on both halves; the `<br>` stays raw because it is this file's markup, not the data. `tests/Feature/Security/OrderListCellEscapingTest.php`, 4 tests / 26 assertions, drives the real AJAX leg with a payload containing none of the middleware's four strings, and checks the name, the mobile and the order reference separately (a sweep that escaped one would have passed the others). **Bitten twice:** removing the two customer `e()` calls returns 2 failures; removing the reference's returns 1. The reference bite was a FALSE reading first — a `sed` pattern that did not match left the file unmodified and the test passed, which looks exactly like a vacuous test. A revert proves nothing until you confirm it is in the file. The third case asserts an ordinary name still renders, so the fix cannot be "escape it into entities on screen". |
| **F43** | **The design-system preview harness granted a permission production does not have, so the screenshots showed the OPPOSITE of the application.** P2-02-13 renamed the dashboard's product gate to `Manage Products` (plural, the real row name); `docs/design-system/tools/fixtures/dashboard-shared.php:92` and `fixtures/components.php:25` still granted `Manage Product`, singular — and the harness answers `isAbleTo()` by exact array-key lookup. So every "permitted merchant" preview render hid the best-selling card, the out-of-stock attention item with its `Review stock` link, and the Products stat link: the harness reproduced the exact defect the rename had just fixed. (The review also named the add-product onboarding step. It is gated on the same name, but its checklist does not render in this fixture state at all because P2-06-1 is held, so it is not counted.) (The review also named the add-product onboarding step; that step is gated on the same name, but the checklist it belongs to does not render in this fixture state at all, because P2-06-1 is held — so it is not counted here.) **159/159 browser checks passed throughout**, because no check asserted those blocks were PRESENT in the granted state. Found by the same review. | **FIXED** — both fixture keys renamed. After re-rendering, the granted preview carries `Best selling products` and a third attention item (`below the stock threshold` + `Review stock`) that had been missing. The production-side ratchet `DashboardPermissionNamesTest` was already sound and reads grantable names from the seeders; it simply does not cover the preview fixtures, which are documentation tooling. Re-ran both suites against freshly rendered fixtures: 159/159 and 66/66. |
| **F44** | **A NULL order status would have read as "Pending".** The P2-04-4 rewrite cast `delivered_status` with `(int)` before the label lookup, and `(int) null` is `0` — so an order whose status was never written would have claimed a fulfilment state it does not have. The markup it replaced rendered an empty cell for the same row, which is less wrong. Raised by the review as a question rather than a defect; it is the same class as F19's epoch dates, so it was not left to a decision. | **FIXED before shipping** — NULL and `''` short-circuit to a neutral badge reading `Status not set`. An unrecognised NON-NULL code still renders `Status :code`, which is the improvement over the old empty cell that the rewrite was for. `tests/Feature/Merchant/OrderStatusLabelTest.php`, 3 tests / 31 assertions; **bitten** (the fallback replaced by `?? ''` → 1 failure). The NULL case itself is NOT driven and says so: the column is NOT NULL in the declared schema, SQLite refuses the write, and a conditionally-skipped test would join the declared skip set the gate asserts. That case asserts the REASON instead — it reads `PRAGMA table_info` and fails if the column ever becomes nullable, naming the test to write then. The guard stays because GATE F records that the migrations are not the schema. |
| **F45** | **The product list's stock badges were the order list's defect on the screen beside it.** `badge bg-light-primary` for "In stock" measured **2.59:1** (white on the merchant's brand green — the same pair as F9) and `bg-light-danger` for "Out of stock" **2.66:1**, in all four of LTR/RTL x light/dark. Both are text and both needed 4.5:1. | **FIXED** — on `ss-badge` through the shared `App\Support\DsBadge`, which `OrderStatusTone::badge()` now delegates to as well, so the badge markup has one definition for every string-building DataTable. `In Variant` also moves from warning to neutral: it is not a stock level and not a problem — it says the stock lives on the variants — and an amber pill in a stock column reads as "something is wrong with this product". |
| **F46** | **ESCALATED, NOT FIXED — the checkout path attributes an order from request input.** `app/Services/PaymentService.php:2689` builds `product_order_id` as `$request->customer_id . date('YmdHis')` for a caller `auth('customers')->check()` has already authenticated, and `:2696` writes the same raw value to `orders.customer_id`. That is CLAUDE.md's most productive defect pattern verbatim — authenticate a principal, then act entirely on request input — and the authenticated customer's own id is available on the line above. Two consequences: a shopper can attribute an order to a value they chose, and the string reaches a `string` column, which is how the order list's reference cell became an XSS vector. The usual objection (the `unsignedBigInteger` insert would fail) does not hold: `config/database.php:55` sets `'strict' => false` on the `mysql` connection, so MySQL coerces `customer_id` to 0 and stores `product_order_id` whole. | **FIXED [2026-10-26] ON THE OWNER'S WRITTEN APPROVAL**, with security and integration tests, in isolated dev/test only. **THE FINDING WAS RECORDED AGAINST ONE FILE AND THE BLOCK EXISTS IN TEN PLACES ACROSS NINE** — a census found it byte-for-byte in `PaymentService`, `AuthorizeNetPaymnetController`, `PayUPaymentController`, `PaymentController` (twice), `NMIPayController`, `KhaltiPaymnetController`, `EasebuzzController`, `PaiementProPaymentController` and `CinetPayController`. Fixing only the file somebody had read would have left the control bypassable by choosing a different payment method, which is the exact mistake F50 records. All ten authenticated branches now take the id from the session; the guest branch is untouched everywhere, because there `$request->customer_id` is the id of the Customer the handler found or created from the billing info. **TWO SITES ARE DELIBERATELY EXEMPT, each verified:** `PosController` authenticates the MERCHANT (`auth()`, gated on `Create Pos`) and the merchant legitimately picks the customer; `WishlistController` replaces the posted id with the session's via `$request->merge()` before any use and fails closed for a non-customer token. **RATCHETED:** `tests/Unit/Payments/OrderAttributionRatchetTest.php` (4 tests) enumerates the exemptions by name and fails on any new copy — it would have caught all ten at once — and was **bitten** by reverting CinetPay, which it then named in all three assertions. **DRIVEN, with its limit stated:** a signed-in shopper posting another customer's id, an injection string, and their own id all produce an order owned by the shopper with no posted string in the reference (14 assertions). **But that route cannot bite the spelling**, measured: reverting `place_order`'s own line still produced the shopper's id, because `ModuleStorefrontInputGuard` (web group, `bootstrap/app.php:274`) rewrites `customer_id` from the session before the handler runs. **SO WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and is not claimed:** whether the nine web/gateway copies were reachable with a hostile `customer_id` at all — that guard is per-route and whether it covers the gateway callbacks was not determined. The fix is correct either way; it is defence in depth of undemonstrated reachability, not a proven exploit closure. |
| **F47** | **The permission-name ratchet was defeatable by a comment, on the half nobody had comment-proofed.** `DashboardPermissionNamesTest` strips comments from the VIEW side — a docblock naming the wrong permission must not satisfy the scan — and read the SEEDER side raw. But the seeder side builds the ALLOWLIST, so a comment anywhere in `app/Models/Utility.php` or any seeder quoting `'Manage Product'` would have ADDED that name to the grantable set and the ratchet would have stopped biting on the exact defect it exists for (P2-02-13). Raised by the tenant/authz review as hardening. | **FIXED** — the allowlist scan strips block and line comments too. **Measured both ways rather than argued:** with a `// probe: 'Manage Product'` comment added to `Utility.php` and the view reverted to the singular, the fixed ratchet FAILS and names the view; the pre-fix scan, driven against the identical state, PASSES. It is the repository's own rule ("strip comment lines from any source-grep ratchet") applied to the half that had been missed — the view side so prose cannot satisfy it, the seeder side so prose cannot widen it. |
| **F48** | **FIXED [2026-10-26] under R2.** ~~RECORDED, NOT FIXED — 29 `env()` calls~~ **The count and the variable list were both wrong, and 12+11+4 = 27 rather than 29 was the tell.** Re-measured: **31 occurrences / 30 live** in `resources/views/`, over **four** variables — `IS_MOBILE` x12, `APP_URL` x10 live, `APP_NAME` x4 and **`default_language` x4, which this row omitted** — plus 3 PHP sites reading the same two flags. Laravel's `env()` reads `$_ENV`, which `php artisan config:cache` does not populate — so on any deployment that caches config (the normal production posture) all 29 evaluate to null. The visible consequences are a HOST-LESS url wherever `APP_URL` is concatenated (the order detail page's "copy link" button copies `/<slug>/order/<cipher>` with no host), a blank application name in titles, and a mobile switch that always reads "desktop". **Found while planning P2-04-6**, from one call on that page. | **NOT FIXED, and the limit of the claim is stated:** what was measured is the 29 call sites and the framework's documented behaviour — **NOT** that this deployment runs `config:cache`. If it does not, none of this is live today; it becomes live the first time somebody caches config, which is a deploy step rather than a code change. The fixes are `config('app.url')`, `config('app.name')` and a config entry for the mobile switch. It crosses many screens, so it is its own slice with a census keyed on the CALL, not on a file list — the four censuses in §4c of the handoff are all about what happens when that is done the other way round. |
| **F49** | **Every order note was attributed to whoever was READING the page.** All five note branches ended `{{ $note->created_at->format(...) }}{{ __(' by ') }}{{ Auth::user()->name }}` — so one note read "by Amina" to Amina and "by Khalid" to Khalid. Visible in the rendered text once the fixture finally produced a note: `"25 Oct 2026 07:06 PM<by>Amina Haddad"`. | **FIXED by removing the claim, because there is no honest name to put in its place.** `order_notes` has NO author column: one migration (`2024_01_26_100237`), no later one, nothing in `_gate_f_pending/`, nothing in any module; `customer_id` is a FK to the SHOPPER while these notes are written by staff. Adding `created_by` is a migration and therefore an owner decision, so the timestamp stands alone. Pinned and **bitten**. The test had to be SCOPED to the notes panel — the layout greets the signed-in user by name once per page, so a body-wide search answered a different question; measured, exactly one occurrence, from the header. |
| **F50** | **A deleted product shifted the Total column.** The items table's header adds `<th>Downloadable Product</th>` when the order is Delivered; the body added the matching `<td>` only when `($product OR $variant)` -- the code uses a logical-or operator; it is spelled OR here because a literal double-pipe splits a markdown table row was ALSO true. A delivered order naming a product that has since been deleted therefore rendered **3 cells against 4 headers**, and every column after the gap displayed under the wrong heading. | **FIXED** — one condition decides both, and what goes INSIDE the cell is the only thing the product's existence affects. Pinned by a test that deletes the product AFTER the order names it (the real sequence) and compares `<td>` count to `<th>` count per row; **bitten** (3 against 4). |
| **F51** | **The order detail page offered another tenant's downloadable file.** The items loop ran `Product::where('id', $item['product_id'])->first()` and `ProductVariant::where('id', …)` PER ITEM with no store predicate. Neither model registers a `StoreScope` and neither is in `config/tenancy.php`'s `scoped_models`, so each returned whichever row held that id — and the only field read is `downloadable_product`, which the view turns into a download link. **Measured, not reasoned:** store A's order naming store B's product id rendered `uploads/<B>/beta-secret-file.zip` to store A's merchant. `product_json` is written at checkout from the cart, and S19 established a cart can carry a foreign product id. | **FIXED** — both lookups hoisted above the loop (two queries instead of two per line item) and scoped to the ORDER's store, which `order_detail()` now returns. **Not the CALLER's store:** the platform operator legitimately opens another store's order (the operator limb is first in both `mayUseOrderScreen()` and `OrderDocumentAccessGuard`), so `getCurrentStore()` here would have blanked the line items for exactly the principal the guard admits. **Bitten** — dropping the predicate puts the foreign path back. Worth noting what already contained this: `StoredPathPolicy` keeps store B's file under store B's folder, so the FILE was never mis-stored; what leaked was this page's willingness to link to it. |
| **F52** | **`target="_blank"` with no `rel`, four on this page and one in the shared layout.** `partision/header.blade.php:135` is the storefront link in the admin header, so EVERY admin screen carried it: the opened tab gets a `window.opener` handle on the admin page and can navigate it, and the WhatsApp links leak the admin url as a referrer. Zero `rel="noopener"` anywhere in either file. | **FIXED**, all five. The test counts `target="_blank"` against `rel=…noopener…` on the RENDERED html and asserts they are equal AND non-zero, so a new bare `_blank` cannot slip in beside the fixed ones and the check cannot pass against a page that stopped opening windows. **Bitten** — removing only the layout's returns `4 links … and 3 carry rel=noopener`, which is also how the layout one was found. |
| **F53** | **`HomeController::top_brand_category_chart()` is MySQL-only SQL, and it 500s on any SQLite instance.** The four tab queries join `orders` with `SUBSTRING_INDEX(SUBSTRING_INDEX(orders.product_id, ',', numbers.n), ',', -1)` against a generated numbers table, and the day/week tabs add `CURDATE()`. SQLite has neither function. | **Recorded, not fixed, and deliberately not claimed either way about production.** Production is MySQL, where both functions exist, so this is **not** evidence of a production defect — and running the admin on the SQLite dev instance is **not** evidence that it works there. It matters here only because it makes the live checks intermittently red: `check-order-detail-live.cjs` reported `500 /top-brand-category-chart` in 1 of 4 runs, which looked like a flake until the log gave the deterministic cause. The endpoint fails on that instance **every time**; what varies is whether its XHR lands inside the window a check is watching. Fixing it means either a portable rewrite of four raw queries or a MySQL dev instance, and neither belongs in a product-list slice. |
| **F54** | **The same chart sums `orders.final_price` — the ORDER total — once per matching product.** The join is `categories → products → orders` on a product id matched against a comma-separated `orders.product_id`, and the aggregate is `SUM(orders.final_price)`, not a line total. So an order containing three of a category's products contributes its **whole total three times** to that category's bar. | **Recorded from reading the query, and the second half is explicitly UNVERIFIED.** The double-counting is an arithmetic property of the SQL and needs no test to see. What is *not* established is whether it can cross tenants: the store predicate sits on `categories`/`product_brands`, and `Order` is not in `scoped_models`, so the only thing confining the sum to one store is that a product belongs to one store. Whether an order's `product_id` list can hold another store's product id has **not been tested**, and this row does not claim it either way. Trustworthy analytics is **Phase 05**; a cross-tenant read would be a security finding and would move. Flagged so the next phase starts from the question rather than from the chart. |
| **F55** | **The `IS_MOBILE` flag now disagrees with the screen that configures it.** `packages/workdo/SidebarCustomization/src/resources/views/super-admin/index.blade.php:178` carries the predicate `$module->name == 'mobilescreensetting' && env('IS_MOBILE') != 'yes'` — byte-for-byte the twin of the two `helper.php` lines R2 moved to `config()`. It is the **only semantic twin** among the 45 remaining vendored Blade `env()` calls (the other 44 are 21 `APP_URL` + 3 `APP_NAME` meta/cosmetic, 19 `DATA_INSERT_APP_THEME`, 1 `SITE_RTL`). On a cached deployment with `IS_MOBILE=yes`, `helper.php` now renders the `mobilescreensetting` row in the real sidebar while this screen still hides it, so the operator sees a sidebar entry they cannot toggle. Found by independent review of R2. Only bites on a deployment that sets `IS_MOBILE=yes`. |

| **F57** | **`$footer_text` in `layouts/guest.blade.php:95` is dead, and the guest layout holds three brand fallbacks for one condition.** The variable is assigned and **never read** anywhere in `resources/`, `themes/`, `app/` or `packages/workdo/`; the footer that actually renders is `:410-416`, which reads `$setting['footer_text']` directly with its own fallback. So the three strings are `'Laravel'` at `:140`, `'Ecommercego saas'` at `:95` (dead) and `'E-CommerceGo'` at `:416` (also dead — `config()`'s second argument fires only when the KEY is absent, and `config/app.php:16` always defines it). `partision/footerlink.blade.php:16` repeats the `:416` form. | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED — cosmetic and pre-existing.** Deliberately not 'fixed' by wiring `$footer_text` into `:416`: that would change the rendered footer. A separate cleanup should delete the dead assignment and pick ONE canonical brand string. Found by independent review of R2, which also notes R2's own change at `:95` is therefore unobservable. |
| **F58** | **A product CANNOT BE CREATED on any database built from the migrations, because GATE F's `products.cost_price` has no migration.** `ProductController::store():131` validates `cost_price` as **required** and `:303` writes `$Product->cost_price` **unconditionally**, but the column exists only in production (`docs/GATE-F-VERIFIED-SCHEMA.md:35`: `decimal(15,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.00`, populated on all 45 products) and its migration is parked at `database/migrations/_gate_f_pending/0004_draft_add_cost_price_to_products_table.php.draft`. **DRIVEN, not reasoned** — a full create through the real UI with every required field and both images answered: `{"flag":"error","msg":"SQLSTATE[HY000]: ... table products has no column named cost_price"}`, store()'s own try/catch handing the raw SQL text to the merchant as a toast. **The suite is structurally blind to it:** no test creates a product through `store()` — every one uses `Product::forceCreate([...])` without `cost_price` — and `GateFReconciliation.php:270` even guards with `if (! $this->hasColumn('products','cost_price'))`. So a fresh install, CI and this dev fixture all share a product editor whose Create cannot work, while production is fine. | **FIXED [2026-10-26] ON THE OWNER'S WRITTEN APPROVAL**, scoped to isolated development and test environments, existing data preserved, no production migration run. The parked draft is promoted to `database/migrations/2026_10_26_000100_add_cost_price_to_products_table.php` with production's own type, `decimal(15,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.00`, read off the dump on 2026-10-24 rather than guessed. **SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY A FLAG:** `up()` returns early when the column already exists, so it cannot alter production even if run there, and `down()` is a deliberate no-op because an earlier version of it dropped the column whenever present and, against a restored copy, destroyed all 45 merchant cost values in 30.28ms. **FOUR SAFETY PROPERTIES MEASURED, not reasoned:** a fresh migrate adds the column; a second run reports *Nothing to migrate*; with the column present and the migration row deleted — the production shape — `up()` ran in 1.40ms and left a planted `12.34` untouched; and `migrate:rollback` ran in 0.14ms and destroyed nothing. **AND THE THING IT UNBLOCKS IS PROVEN END TO END:** `docs/development/phase-2/tools/check-product-create-live.cjs` drives a real create — name, category and tag through their actual Choices and select2 widgets, a cover image through the file input and a gallery image through `dropzone.addFile()` — and gets **9/9** with the product listed afterwards. **Bitten:** dropping the column again reproduces `table products has no column named cost_price` verbatim, and re-running the migration restores 9/9. **MySQL evidence is PREPARED AND UNCOLLECTED:** `tests/Support/Security/bin/verify-cost-price-migration-on-mysql.php` (registered with the runner) establishes the exact `decimal(15,2)`, the engine-enforced scale, the INSERT-time ER_BAD_NULL_ERROR, the production-shaped no-op and the harmless rollback — none of which SQLite can answer, since it reports any decimal as `numeric` — and this container has no MySQL server. That gap is named here rather than papered over. The draft is removed from `_gate_f_pending/` and the promotion is recorded at the top of that directory's README; the other eight drafts stay parked because their types are still guesses. |
| **F59** | **`ProductController::update()` wrote NULL into the NOT NULL `products.track_stock`, so every product edit on a store with stock management OFF was a bare 500.** `store():309-316` guards both branches (`?? 0`, `!empty(...) ? ... : 0`); `update():1340-1347` guarded **neither**, and its `else` branch is reached exactly when the value is absent — which is always, on a store whose `stock_management` setting is off, because the control is then never rendered. `update()` has no try/catch, so there was no message either. **Measured:** `POST /product/32` → **500**, `SQLSTATE[23000]: NOT NULL constraint failed: products.track_stock`. | **FIXED [2026-10-26]** by mirroring `store()`'s own guard, one line per branch. **It changes no value production stores:** `config/database.php:55` sets `'strict' => false` on the mysql connection, which coerces the NULL to the same 0 this now writes explicitly — which is also why it was invisible in production and fatal on SQLite and on any strict-mode MySQL (Laravel's own default). Found by driving the editor end to end, not by reading. |

| **F60** | **WITHDRAWN before it was recorded as a defect: "a rejected product save tells the merchant nothing".** Driving a save that the server rejected, I measured `toastr` undefined, `#toast-container` absent and `show_toastr()` producing no element — and was one step from recording a silent-failure defect on every product save. It is wrong. `public/js/custom.js:230` does not use toastr at all: it calls `notifier.show(...)`, and the real node is `div.notifier.danger.shown`, which **is** rendered and **is** visible with the message in it. The lesson is the one CLAUDE.md already states about censuses: a probe keyed on the library you expected finds nothing and reads as absence. | **NOT A DEFECT.** Kept as a row because the wrong probe is the reusable mistake, and because `check-advanced-fields-live.cjs` now asserts on `.notifier` with that reasoning written beside it. |
| **F61** | **SEVEN NULL-into-NOT-NULL writes of F59's exact class, found in three rounds, now closed by a ratchet rather than an eighth fix.** `:1276` `$product->stock_status = $request->stock_status;` is unguarded, `products.stock_status` is `string NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, and the field is **not in the validator**; `product/edit.blade.php` renders its three radios with **no `checked` fallback**, so a product created by a path that never posts it (ImportExport, Shopify, `Api\DashboardController`) opens the editor with none selected, and a merchant who saves without clicking one wrote NULL. `:1464` `$product->product_stock = $request->product_stock;` is the same shape on an `integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` column — a browser posts `''`, which coerces, but a client that omits the key with `track_stock=1` wrote NULL. A column DEFAULT does not rescue an explicit NULL. | **FIXED [2026-10-26]**, one coalesce each: `stock_status` to the stored value (the radio group has no empty option, so absent means "not posted", never "cleared", and preserving cannot lose data), `product_stock` to 0. **[2026-10-26] FIVE MORE, and then a RATCHET.** Reviewing the `stock_status`/`product_stock` fix found **`trending`** at `update():1356` -- same shape, in neither rule set, and only latent because the edit form carries a hidden `trending=0` partner. Scanning for the whole class then found four more: **`trending` twice in `store()`** (`:296`, `:601`, and `trending` is not in store()'s validator either), **`sale_price` twice** (`store():309`, `update():1489`), and **`stock_status` in `store()`**. The `store()` ones are the worse half: those are INSERTs, where an explicit NULL is ER_BAD_NULL_ERROR on MySQL *whatever* the sql_mode -- there is no silent coercion to fall back on. `update():1489`'s `sale_price` was kept non-NULL only by `$request->sale_price = $request->sale_price ? ... : 0;` three hundred lines earlier, which works because neither Illuminate's nor Symfony's Request defines `__set`, so it creates a dynamic property that later reads find before `__get` -- and emits a PHP 8.3 deprecation. **ALL SEVEN ARE FIXED, and the class is now ratcheted:** `tests/Unit/Schema/ProductNotNullWriteRatchetTest.php` reads the NOT NULL columns out of the products migration and fails on any assignment from request input into one of them that is neither guarded on the line, guarded by a condition on the same request key above it, nor validated `required`. It would have caught all seven at once. Four of them are also driven end to end by `ProductUpdateNullGuardsTest`, which posts the payload with one key REMOVED -- the shape no existing test had, because `ProductConcurrentEditTest::payload()` carries every key and its own comment says that is *because* omitting them is a 500: the suite had measured the defect, worked around it in a fixture, and moved on. Each fix was bitten individually. **The guards are deliberately not uniform** -- `trending` and `stock_status` coalesce to the STORED value (their controls cannot be omitted by a browser, and for `stock_status` that is also fail-closed, since production's `''` made `'' == 'out_of_stock'` false and the product silently purchasable), while `track_stock`, `product_stock` and `sale_price` coalesce to 0, which is what production's non-strict UPDATE already stored and what `store()` writes for the same input; changing a stock quantity's behaviour would be a commercial change, not this slice's to make. The asymmetry is recorded in the test's own provider. Found across three rounds of independent review, each of which found the previous round's siblings. |
| **F62** | **Two of the four places `ProductController` creates a Tag never set the NOT NULL `tags.slug`.** `store():356` and `update():1521` set it with `Tag::slugs($tag)`; the two **variant** branches, `store():639` and `update():1696`, did not, so the column was omitted from the INSERT — a 500 on SQLite (dev and the whole suite) and, on production MySQL with `strict` off, a row silently stored with `slug = ''`, two of which collide on every storefront tag route. **It became reachable from the UI only through the F16 fix**: with select2 loading, the layout's one global `$('.select2').select2({tags: true, createTag: ...})` turns the product tag field into a free-text control, and a merchant-typed tag is exactly the unresolved value these branches insert. Measured: before, `#tag_id` was a plain `<select multiple>` accepting only its three seeded options; after, a typed value serialises as `tag_id[]=zz-brand-new-tag`. | **FIXED [2026-10-26]** — two lines, copying the guard the sibling branches already had, and now ratcheted: a test asserts every `new Tag()` in the controller sets a slug before `save()`, which holds for all four sites and any fifth. **TWO MORE DEFECTS IN THE SAME METHOD, found by reviewing the fix (F62b):** `Tag::slugs()` loops `$i = 1..100` looking for a free suffix and then **falls off the end returning NULL** -- which the callers assign straight into the NOT NULL column, so the 101st tag sharing a base name was the exact 500 F62 had just closed, moved a hundred rows down the road; and the pool is **cross-tenant**, because `getRelatedSlugs()` uses the query builder and `Tag` is not in `scoped_models`, so one store exhausting `sale-1..100` broke every other tenant's first `sale` tag. **THAT SECOND HALF WAS ALREADY KNOWN AND IS DELIBERATE, which this row first failed to say.** `ResidualTenancyExposureTest::test_the_raw_query_entry_points_in_the_owned_models_are_exactly_these_twelve` censuses all five copies of this helper by `path:line` and records at length why a `store_id` predicate is NOT added: per-store slugs would let two stores mint the same product slug, and whether the storefront could still resolve one then depends on every slug lookup across `app/Http/**` and `themes/**` also carrying a store predicate. **This fix adds no predicate and preserves that decision**; what is new is only that an exhausted pool returned NULL. Promoting it shifted the censused line and FAILED that test -- caught by the known-outcome gate rather than by me, and corrected with the reason written beside the census entry. Separately, a name made only of characters its regex strips (`!!!`, an emoji, a lone `+`) reduced to `''`, which inserts because it is not NULL but is not a slug either and two of them are indistinguishable -- reachable since the tag field became free text. Both fixed: an empty slug falls back to `tag`, and the suffix search falls through to a random suffix in a `while` that makes it exact rather than merely improbable. Driven by a test that plants all 101 rows. **One concern was CHECKED AND REFUTED:** that making the slug always-present could turn a loud crash into a quiet cross-tenant collision. It cannot -- `grep` across `app/`, `resources/`, `themes/`, `routes/` and `packages/workdo/` finds **no reader of `tags.slug` at all**; the column is write-only today and its only consumer is `Tag::slugs()`'s own uniqueness probe. The first version of the fix's comment claimed the `''` rows "collide on the storefront tag routes"; there are no such routes, and the claim is corrected. Found by independent review of the F16 fix, asked specifically what that fix made newly reachable. |

Every consumer initialises explicitly, so it costs nothing. Found by independent review. |

Measured on the same element: before the F16 fix `getBoundingClientRect()` was 535.5 × 41.375; after, **1 × 1** (`select2-hidden-accessible`). `checkValidity()` is false in both states and the submit is blocked in both — what changes is that the browser can no longer anchor a validation bubble to a 1×1 clipped control, so the merchant gets a dead "Create Order" button with no explanation instead of "Please select an item in the list." | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** The honest fix is either to drop `required` and lean on the server rules, or to have the layout's global select2 init skip `required` controls — the second is a change to every `.select2` on every admin page and does not belong in this slice. | **F68** | **The disclosure's hint failed AA in dark mode, at 3.85:1, in the slice that measured contrast in four configurations.** `.ss-advanced__hint` is a `<p>`, and the host's dark stylesheet does `.dataTable-info, p { color: #808191 !important; }` (`assets/css/style-dark.css:29442`). So the token set on it lost, and the hint computed to `rgb(128,129,145)` on the dark card -- 3.85:1 at 12px, against the 4.5:1 it needs -- while the summary two lines above read 6.79:1. **The DS file already had the mechanism and the warning:** a block headed *Host override defence* listing the five classes that may sit on a `<p>` and restating their colour with `!important`. `.ss-advanced__hint` was a sixth and was not on the list. **My own check missed it by sampling `getComputedStyle(summary).color` and nothing else** -- two text nodes in the component, one measured, and the pass was reported for the whole thing. | **FIXED [2026-10-26]** by adding the class to the existing defence list: now 6.79:1 dark, 5.23:1 light. Bitten by removing it again, which reproduces 3.85:1 exactly in both dark configurations. The live harness now measures the hint in all four, so it cannot regress silently. Found by independent review of P2-03-8. |
| **F69** | **The verification for P2-03-8 was itself defective in five ways, and two of its tests could not fail at all.** Found by independent review, and this is the row worth reading twice. **(1)** `AdvancedFieldsDisclosureTest::slotOf()` returned the region starting at the component's OPENING TAG, which carries `:fields="['tax_id[]', 'tax_status', 'brand_id', 'label_id', 'shipping_id', 'product_weight']"` -- so all six names were in the string whether or not a single control was inside the disclosure, and `test_the_six_catalogue_fields_are_inside_the_disclosure` **could not fail**. The helper strips Blade comments precisely so no assertion can be satisfied by the prose explaining it, and was then satisfied by the component's own argument list. **(2)** `test_the_required_fields_stay_outside_the_disclosure` searched for the quoted PHP literal `'category_id'`, but the create view writes that control as raw HTML `<select name="category_id">` -- so moving Category into the disclosure would have passed that test AND the required-attribute test beside it, leaving a server-side-required field collapsed: exactly what it exists to prevent. **(3)** `inventory-product-fields.cjs` CAPTURED `readonly`, `tag`, `type` and `multiple` and compared none of them -- and `readonly` is a live axis here, since the variant toggle runs `$('.product_price input').prop('readOnly', true)`; a `<select name="brand_id">` swapped for `<input type="hidden">` with the same value passed the entire gate. **(4)** The same gate had **no notion of where a field lives**, which is the one dimension this slice changes: a NEW field added inside the disclosure carrying `required` was printed as `note field ADDED` and **passed**. **(5)** `check-advanced-fields-live.cjs` hardcoded `newWeight = '7.77'` and then saved it, so the fixture came to hold 7.77 and that assertion passed on every later run whether or not the save wrote the field -- a case that could not fail after its own first run. | **ALL FIVE FIXED [2026-10-26].** `slotOf()` now starts past the opening tag and asserts it no longer contains `:fields`; the six fields are matched by CONTROL SHAPE (`name="tax_id[]"`, `Form::select('tax_status'`) not bare names; the required-outside check matches `name="x"` as well as `'x'`; the gate compares readonly/tag/type/multiple and records `insideDetails`, with a STANDING invariant that `required && insideDetails` is never true, checked against the after-snapshot alone so it covers fields the baseline never had; the weight is derived from the stored value and asserted different. The gate went **502 -> 1048 checks**, and the three new comparisons were bitten individually (new required field inside, readonly flip, select->hidden swap). |
| **F70** | **Owner condition 2's "a validation error must OPEN the relevant section" is NOT satisfied for a server-rejected save, and the component's comment claimed it was.** Two independent reasons, both verified: `ProductController` **never calls `withErrors()`** -- `store():155-161` and `update():1228-1233` return `['flag' => 'error', 'msg' => ...]` as JSON for the editor's AJAX handler to toast -- so `$errors` is empty on every render of both product pages; and of the six fields the section owns, four carry **no validation rule at all** and `brand_id`/`label_id` are `nullable`, so none of them can fail in the first place. What a merchant sees when a save is rejected is a `notifier` toast with the first message, and the section stays closed. | **CLOSED [2026-10-26] ON THE OWNER'S WRITTEN APPROVAL of F70.** ~~THE CAPABILITY IS BUILT, TESTED AND CORRECT; ITS REACHABILITY IS AN OPEN GAP.~~ **BOTH HALVES OF THE GAP ARE NOW FIXED.** (1) `ProductController` adds an `errors` key, keyed by field, to both validation-failure responses -- **additive**, with `flag` and `msg` byte-identical, so no existing reader changes. (2) `StoreSyriaDS.applyFormErrors()` applies it: it marks each failed control `is-invalid` and `aria-invalid`, writes an inline message beside it (after the Choices/select2 wrapper where one owns the control, or the note would be hidden alongside the native element), sets `aria-describedby`, builds the summary with `role="alert"`, and **opens the `<details>` the failed field lives in**, flagging it and appending the textual *Needs attention* badge. (3) `product_weight` gained `nullable`, `numeric` and `min:0` in all four rule sets -- because none of the six fields the disclosure holds had any rule, so nothing in that section could fail even in principle and the section-opening was untestable. The column is nullable, so an empty weight stays valid exactly as before. **FLUTTER COMPATIBILITY IS NOT AT RISK, and that is measured rather than assumed:** the shipped app posts product writes to `api/admin/addproduct` and `/updateproduct` (`StoreSyria_app98/lib/utils/base_api.dart:3,57,85`), which are `Api\DashboardController` -- a different handler on a different route. The web routes changed here are reached only by this editor's own jQuery, and the Contract suite asserts nothing about them. **DRIVEN, 28/28** (`check-form-errors-live.cjs`): with the section CLOSED, a negative weight is written, the save is refused, and the section opens with the field on screen, marked, described and summarised, while the existing toast still fires; a corrected value then saves and clears all of it. **Bitten twice** -- removing the server's `errors` key, and neutering the section-opening -- each failing exactly its own cases. One harness defect was found and recorded: `'abc'` assigned to an `<input type="number">` is **rejected by the browser**, so the value stayed empty, `nullable` accepted it, the save succeeded and the case measured nothing; `-5` is a value the input accepts and `min:0` refuses. The two Feature tests that hand the component a `ViewErrorBag` directly still stand, and are still the shape that cannot distinguish a working capability from a reachable one -- which is why the live harness above exists beside them rather than instead of them. The four comments that stated the server-side path as live behaviour remain corrected: it is the AJAX path, not the `$errors` path, that a merchant actually meets here. Found by independent review; closed on the owner's approval. |

| **F71** | **In `ApiController::ordersave()` the authenticated customer's order reference is DEAD CODE — the line below overwrites it unconditionally.** `:8944` sets `$product_order_id = $customer_id . date('YmdHis')` inside `if (auth('customers')->check())`, and `:8948`, two lines later and OUTSIDE the branch, sets `$product_order_id = '0' . date('YmdHis')`. So every order placed through `order-save`, signed in or not, carries the GUEST reference form. `place_order()` does it correctly at `:3786`, which is how the two drifted apart unnoticed. Found while censusing F46 — the census asked "which of these read request input", and this one answered "none, and also none of it runs". | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** The reference is what a merchant and a customer both see on an order, and `orders.product_order_id` is joined on by `order_billing_details`, `order_coupon_details` and `order_tax_details` (`PaymentService:2867,2896,2941,2994`). Changing its format for authenticated API orders is an **order-behaviour change**, which the owner's standing limits put behind approval — so it is recorded with its line numbers rather than quietly corrected. Low severity: the reference is still unique (the timestamp carries that) and nothing is mis-attributed; what is lost is that the reference no longer identifies the customer, and that `is_guest` and the reference now disagree. |
| **F72** | **A product variant key reaches the DOM as a CSS SELECTOR, so any merchant-typed term containing `/`, `.`, `#` or `"` broke the variant editor in two separate ways.** `ProductController` builds the key from the merchant's own attribute terms -- `$str .= '-' . str_replace(' ', '', $item)` -- and **only spaces are stripped**; `product_attribute_options.terms` is validated `required` and nothing else, so `M/L`, `Black/White` or `2.5"` is an ordinary thing to type. **(a) Selectors this code builds.** `attribute_combinations_data.blade.php` and `attribute_combinations_edit.blade.php` concatenate the key into `$('.down_product_' + key)`, `.product_weights_`, `.manageble_stock_` and `.remove_option_`. jQuery answers `Syntax error, unrecognized expression: .down_product_M/L-Natural` and **throws**, which aborts the handler -- so the downloadable / virtual / manage-stock toggles stopped working for **every** variant on the page, not just the one with the odd term. **(b) An identifier Bootstrap resolves.** `data-bs-target="#collapseone_<key>"` is handed to `document.querySelector()` **inside Bootstrap's collapse plugin**, which threw `'#collapseone_M/L' is not a valid selector` -- so the merchant **could not open that variant's panel at all** and its price, SKU and stock were unreachable. The request-key suffixes a few lines away already carry `str_replace('.', '_', $str)`: somebody met the `.` case and patched it there and not here. **And the sibling CREATE view was already fixed.** `attribute_combinations.blade.php:21` has carried `$safe_str = preg_replace('/[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]/', '_', $str)` since before this change -- so this defect was found once, fixed in **one of three** sibling views, and left in the other two. That is the shape of F46 and F50 again. | **FIXED [2026-10-26], both halves, each bitten independently.** The two fixes are deliberately *different*, because the failures are. **(a) is escaped at the call site**, not sanitised: a new `ssSelectorKey()` helper (`CSS.escape`, with a `[^\w-]` regex fallback) wraps the value at all four sites in the data view and three in the edit view. Escaping cannot collide, so two distinct keys stay distinct. **(b) must sanitise the identifier**, because the call site is Bootstrap's and no escape of ours is reached -- so the collapse trio (`id`, `data-bs-target`, `aria-controls`) now uses the same `$safe_str` the create view already had, kept character-for-character identical on purpose so there is one spelling of this fix rather than two. **`$str` is untouched wherever it is a VALUE** -- request field names, `data-id`, and the label the merchant reads -- because sanitising a value would change what is submitted and what is displayed. **DRIVEN 8/8** through the real editor (`check-variant-key-escaping-live.cjs`), by the merchant's own path: touch the attribute picker, tick *enable variation*, choose terms including `M/L`, regenerate, then open the panel and delete the combination. **Bitten twice, each failing only its own cases:** reverting the identifier gives `'#collapseone_M/L' is not a valid selector` and the panel will not open (3 cases); reverting the escape gives `Syntax error, unrecognized expression: .remove_option_M/L` **and the combination is not removed** -- `before=1 after=1` (2 cases). **THE FOURTH SITE WAS NEARLY MISSED, and the way it hides is worth recording:** the data view ships a document-delegated `.delete_option` handler and **renders no `.delete_option` button**, so the file reads as self-consistently dead code. It is live -- `edit.blade.php:741` calls `attribute_option_data()` on `$(document).ready()`, so that view loads on **every** edit page load and its handler binds to `document`; replacing the container's HTML later does not unbind it, and the button that fires it comes from the sibling view with the **raw** key. Both handlers run on one click. **Ratcheted** by `tests/Unit/Merchant/VariantKeySelectorSafetyTest.php` across all three sibling views, because the ordinary next event is somebody pasting one of these blocks into a fourth. **Its own first two versions could not do the job and the bites are what showed it** -- see F74. A `M/L` term is now **permanent in the dev fixture** as a canary, so a page-error check exercises this instead of rediscovering it. |
| **F73** | **`$safe_str` can COLLIDE: two variant terms differing only in a special character reduce to one identifier.** `preg_replace('/[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]/', '_', $str)` maps both `M/L` and `M.L` to `M_L`, so a product carrying both terms renders **two accordion items with the same `id`** and the same `remove_option_` class. Bootstrap's collapse resolves `#collapseone_M_L` to the first match, so one header opens the other variant's panel; and `$('.remove_option_M_L').remove()` removes **both**. Found by reviewing the F72 fix rather than by a failure. | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED — and the F72 fix deliberately inherits it.** The collision is a property of the sanitiser that `attribute_combinations.blade.php` has used since before this change; F72 spread it to the other two views on purpose, because the alternative was a **third** spelling of one fix across three sibling views, and a per-row index or hash would change identifiers the create view already emits. **Severity: low and bounded.** It needs one product carrying two terms that differ *solely* in characters the regex strips -- `M/L` and `M.L`, not `M/L` and `L` -- and the harm is confined to the editor's own accordion; nothing is mis-saved, because the submitted field names use the raw `$str` and stay distinct. The collision-free fix is to make the identifier a function of the row index as well as the key, in all three views at once, with the create view's `data-original-id` path re-driven. That is a self-contained slice and is better done as one change than folded into F72. |
| **F74** | **My own F72 ratchet passed against live defects, twice, for two opposite reasons — and only the bite found it.** Version 1 asked whether the variable concatenated into a selector was assigned from `ssSelectorKey()` **anywhere in the file**. `downProductId` is declared at three separate sites, so reverting **one** of them left the other two to vouch for it: the test stayed green while the defect was live and reproducible in the browser. Version 2 over-corrected and demanded that **every** assignment of the name be safe -- but these are Blade files and the name in the fourth site is `id`, so `\bid\s*=` also matched every HTML `id="..."` attribute and the `var id = $(this).attr('data-url')` in unrelated handlers. It flagged **nineteen innocent lines** and failed against the correct tree. | **FIXED [2026-10-26].** The rule is now the one JavaScript actually applies: for each selector site, take the **nearest `var`/`let`/`const` declaration of that name that precedes it** and judge that one. Requiring the declaration keyword is what excludes the HTML attributes; taking the nearest preceding one is what stops a sibling site vouching for a reverted one. **Bitten five ways, all five failing and the restored tree passing:** the collapse identifier reverted in the edit view; the **first** of three `downProductId` escapes; the **last** of the three (so it is not merely the first occurrence being judged); the fourth site's own escape; and the create view's PHP sanitiser weakened to `$safe_str = $str` (so its exemption is conditional on the sanitiser really being a `preg_replace`, not on the file's name). **This is the project's own stricter TDD rule earning its place for the third time this phase** -- after F69, where two P2-03-8 tests could not fail, and the F46 attribution test, which was vacuous twice. In all three the test was written after the fix and looked right; reverting the fix is the only thing that distinguished a real ratchet from a rubber stamp. |
| **F75** | **A gallery image the server REFUSED was dropped in silence, and the editor advertised three different upload limits, none of them the real one.** Two halves, both measured. **(a) The editor told the merchant nothing true.** The Dropzone is configured `acceptedFiles: a ? null : "image/*"` and the gallery element carries `data-dropzone-multiple`, so it took the **`null`** branch and accepted **any file type at all**, with Dropzone's default 256 MB ceiling; `cover_image`, `downloadable_product` and `preview_video` carried no `accept` attribute. Meanwhile the server enforces `mimes:`/`max:` built from platform settings (`local_storage_validation` = `jpg,jpeg,png,csv,svg,pdf`), and **PHP** caps every file at `upload_max_filesize` = 2 MB and the whole request at `post_max_size` = 8 MB. So three numbers disagreed -- 256 MB advertised, 2 GB configured, 2 MB actually enforced -- and a GIF, WebP or AVIF was accepted by the editor and refused by the server. The dropzone prompt "Drop files here to upload" was also **hardcoded English**. **(b) And the refusal was never reported.** `ProductController`'s COVER path checks the returned flag and errors; its GALLERY loop asked only `isset($pathss['url'])` and **skipped** the rejection, at all three call sites, while `$msg['msg']` had already been set to "Product Successfully Created". MEASURED on the real database: two files queued, **one** `product_images` row written, nothing on disk for the other, **no error and no toast**. The merchant is told it worked and the image is gone. | **FIXED [2026-10-26]. Driven 17/17 (`check-gallery-upload-feedback-live.cjs`), each of the three fixes bitten independently.** **(a)** A new read-only `App\Services\UploadLimits` reads the same settings `Utility::upload_file()` reads and takes the **minimum against PHP's own `upload_max_filesize`**, so the editor states the limit a merchant actually hits. Both views now set `accept` from the server's list **verbatim** -- narrowing it would newly block a `pdf` or `csv` that works today -- set `acceptedFiles` and a real `maxFilesize`, gain `addRemoveLinks` (a queued file could previously only be un-queued by reloading and losing the form), and render a translated hint naming the formats and both limits. Measured: `acceptedFiles` is now the server's list, `maxFilesize` is 2 not 256, and **a GIF is refused before any upload**. **(b)** The three loops now record each refusal and `store()`/`update()` report them beside the success -- `flag` stays `success` because the product really was saved, `msg` gains a `text-danger` line naming the file and the reason, and an additive `rejected_images` key carries it machine-readably. **Flutter is unaffected and that is measured, not assumed:** the shipped app posts product writes to `api/admin/addproduct` (`Api\DashboardController`), a different handler on a different route, and the Contract suite stayed 26/26. **MY FIRST TWO ATTEMPTS AT (b) MEASURED NOTHING, and the harness is what caught both.** The first appended the warning beside the loop under `isset($msg['msg'])`: that guard is false there, because the earlier message assignment only runs `if ($webhook)`, and the final assignment would have overwritten it anyway -- the response came back `{"flag":"success","msg":"Product saved successfully."}` with the warning nowhere in it. The second fixed the server but still showed nothing, because **`create.blade.php` has TWO near-identical save handlers** chosen by `module_is_active('CartQuantityControl')` and I patched the module-only one; the default path navigated away without displaying `data.msg` at all. `edit.blade.php` has the same pair, and its **pre-existing** `localStorage.setItem('success_msg', ...)` sits on the same module-only branch -- so by default an edit save had been showing the merchant no message at all, which this fixes too. Splitting the assertion into B1 (what the server returns) and B2 (what the merchant sees) is what localised it. **Contrast caught a third defect:** the gallery hint read **2.18:1** in both dark configurations, because `.dropzone` computes `rgb(255,255,255)` in **both** themes and the theme-switching `--ss-fg-muted` goes light-grey on it -- while the cover hint, on the real card, read 7.11:1. Same class, same token, two surfaces: the F8 lesson again. Pinned to the primitive `--ss-grey-600` (defined once, never themed; 5.94:1 on white). **56 measured, 0 below AA**, and 26/26 bilingual checks -- Arabic RTL light and dark at 1440 and 390, no placeholder leak, no horizontal overflow. Ratcheted by `tests/Unit/Merchant/UploadLimitsTest.php` (19 tests). |
| **F76** | **`.dropzone` ignores dark mode: it computes `background-color: rgb(255, 255, 255)` in both themes.** Found while contrast-checking F75's hints -- the one inside the dropzone measured 2.18:1 in dark while its twin on the real card measured 7.11:1, and the cause was the surface, not the token. The host's dark stylesheet themes the card and leaves this panel white. | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED, and F75 works around it rather than hiding it.** The hint inside the panel is pinned to a primitive that does not theme-switch, which is correct for text on a known-light surface and is commented as such. Fixing the PANEL is a different and much larger change: `.dropzone` is the upload surface for every module in the platform, so giving it a themed background alters screens far outside the product editor and needs its own contrast pass across all of them. Severity: cosmetic but real -- a dark-mode merchant meets one glaring white panel in an otherwise dark editor, and **any future text placed inside it will hit the same trap**, which is the actual reason to record it rather than leave the knowledge in one CSS comment. |
| **F77** | **The FLUTTER product-write path has F75's defect in a worse form: a refused gallery image writes a `product_images` row with a NULL path instead of being skipped.** Found while verifying that F75 could not affect Flutter. The shipped app posts to `api/admin/addproduct` and `/updateproduct` -> `Api\DashboardController::AddProduct():2369` and `UpdateProduct():2527`, which use the SAME `Utility::keyWiseUpload_file()` helper and then do: `$ProductImage->image_path = $pathss['url'] ?? null;` ... `$ProductImage->save();` -- **unconditionally**. So where the web path silently SKIPPED a refused file, the API path silently CREATES a broken row. `StoredPathPolicy::isSafe(null)` returns true by design (its first branch), so the `ProductImage::saving` hook does not catch it, and the storefront and the app then render an image with no source. `UpdateProduct()` is worse again: it deletes all existing rows first (`ProductImage::where('product_id', ...)->delete()`), so an update whose uploads are refused replaces a working gallery with a set of empty rows. Also noted: `product_image` is validated `required\|array` with **no per-item rule**, so nothing checks the items' type before the helper's own internal `mimes:` does. **NOT a tenancy defect, and that was checked rather than assumed:** `$ProductImage->store_id = $request->store_id` looks like the caller-supplied-predicate pattern, but `AddProduct()` opens with `$this->resolveOwnedStore($request->store_id)` and errors when it is empty, so the value is ownership-validated before use. | **RECORDED, NOT YET FIXED — deliberately held out of the F75 commit.** The fix is not symmetric with the web one and deserves its own slice with its own evidence: not writing a NULL-path row is a data-shape change on a **published API** that the shipped app consumes, so it needs the Contract suite driven against the real app's payloads, and `UpdateProduct()`'s delete-then-insert ordering needs deciding separately (a refusal there should probably not have destroyed the old gallery at all, which is a bigger question than reporting the refusal). Reporting the refusals in the API response is additive and safe; suppressing the broken row is the part that needs the measurement. **Severity: higher than F75**, because the row persists and is rendered, and because the update path can destroy a working gallery. This is the FOURTH time this phase that a defect found on one surface turned out to exist on its sibling -- F30 and P2-05-1 record the same thing in the opposite direction, where the API path had been fixed and the web path left open. |
| **F78** | **DECISION REQUESTED — gallery image REORDER cannot be delivered inside P2-03-4, and the reason is not the column.** `product_images` is a managed table (it has a migration, so this is not GATE F) and has no `sort_order`; adding one is routine. What is not routine is that **nothing in the codebase orders gallery images at all** -- insertion order by `id` is the de-facto order -- so a column that nobody reads would let a merchant reorder images and see nothing change. Making the order real means updating **every reader**, and a census found about twelve files across four surfaces: the web controller, **the Flutter API** (`Api\DashboardController`, `Api\ApiController` -- so the order of the images the shipped app receives would change, which is an API behaviour change), the storefront/theme views, and **three vendored WorkDo modules** (`AuctionProduct`, `ProductsPDF`, `DigitalProduct`). Two of those are the blocker: `packages/workdo/` must not be edited, so a complete feature is impossible without either breaking that rule or accepting that those surfaces ignore the merchant's chosen order. **Note what is NOT a gap:** "choose the primary image" is already solved by a different mechanism -- `products.cover_image_path` is a dedicated field the merchant sets separately, and the edit view previews it (verified; an earlier survey reported it missing, which was a **fixture** gap -- the dev products had `cover_image_path = NULL`, now seeded). And DELETE of an existing gallery image already works: each image renders with its own `.deleteRecord` control (measured on product 1). | **DEFERRED TO PHASE 03 BY OWNER DECISION [2026-10-26] — see `PHASE-02-DECISION-REGISTER.md` D-06.** The owner approved deferring gallery image reorder **across all channels** to Phase 03, to be implemented with **full parity between the storefront, the web merchant UI, Flutter and the WorkDo add-ons, without editing the vendored module files**. **Recorded as a scope-limited deferral, NOT as an implemented feature.** Two things the owner ruled out by name, both verified in code rather than asserted: **(1) no unused `sort_order` column** -- `product_images` still has exactly `id, product_id, image_path, image_url, store_id, created_at, updated_at`, and a column nothing reads would let a merchant reorder and see nothing change; **(2) no web-only ordering that differs from Flutter** -- measured, **0** sites anywhere in `app/`, `themes/` or `resources/` order the gallery, so insertion order by `id` remains the de-facto order on every surface and the web and the app continue to agree. The analysis that produced the deferral stands as the reason it is a Phase 03 shape: the column is the easy part, and the gallery is read in about twelve files across four surfaces including the Flutter API and **three vendored WorkDo modules** (`AuctionProduct`, `ProductsPDF`, `DigitalProduct`) that must not be edited -- so a complete feature was impossible here without either breaking that rule or shipping exactly the surface-to-surface disagreement the owner ruled out. **What is NOT deferred and is still owed in Phase 02:** the rest of P2-03-4 -- upload, deletion, the primary image, the variant experience and validation errors -- each verified by driving it. **And what was never a gap:** "choose the primary image" is already solved by a different mechanism (`products.cover_image_path`, a dedicated field the merchant sets separately, previewed on the edit page); an earlier survey reported it missing and that was a **fixture** gap, since seeded. |
| **F79** | **The merchant list's stock badge does not agree with what a customer can actually DO, and after P2-03-9 it still does not.** `Cart.php:135` refuses a purchase only when `product_stock <= out_of_stock_threshold` **AND** `stock_order_status == 'not_allow'` (`Api\ApiController:454` is the same rule for the app). `stock_order_status` takes `not_allow`, `notify_customer` or `allow`, and **in the fixture and in production it is the empty string** -- which is none of the three, so `'' == 'not_allow'` is false and the threshold check **never fires**. The consequence: a tracked product at zero stock shows "Out of stock" on the merchant list while a customer can still add it to the cart and buy it. F61 recorded the same `''` behaving as a permissive value for `stock_status`; this is its sibling on the purchasing path. | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED, and P2-03-9 deliberately did not try to fix it.** Two reasons. **(1) The honest fix is a commercial decision, not a label.** Either the cart should treat `''` as `not_allow` -- which would stop sales that currently complete, i.e. a change to purchasing behaviour, reserved by the owner's standing limits -- or the badge should say "still sellable" at zero stock, which tells the truth but reads like a defect to a merchant who expects "Out of stock" to mean out of stock. **(2) P2-03-9's acceptance names a different reference**: it asks the indicator to agree with "the product list's own predicate", which is the filter, and that is now true by construction. Conflating purchasability into the same badge would have made it disagree with the filter again. What P2-03-9 does do about it: every badge now carries its rule in a `title`, so the number and the threshold behind the word are visible rather than implied -- which is the part a merchant can act on without a commercial decision being taken on their behalf. **Severity: medium**, and it is a reporting defect rather than a money defect -- no order is mispriced and no stock is mis-counted; a merchant can be misled about whether a line is still selling. |
| **F80** | **The product preview panel hides a declared out-of-stock on an untracked product.** `product/partials/preview.blade.php:23` renders `__('Not tracked')` with a **neutral** tone for any product with `track_stock = 0`, and stops there -- so it never reads `stock_status`. But `stock_status` on an untracked product is not a number and not fiction: it is the merchant's own explicit declaration, and **the cart honours it** (`Cart.php:135`: `track_stock == 0 && stock_status == 'out_of_stock'` refuses the purchase). So a merchant who marked an untracked product out of stock sees a quiet grey "Not tracked" in the preview, with no indication that the product is unbuyable. The list badge does report it. Found by checking whether the preview panel was a third copy of the stock rule -- it is, and on the TRACKED path it agrees with the filter exactly (`<= 0` out, `<= low` warning), which is how P2-03-9 established that the list badge was the only disagreeing surface. | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED — this is a judgement call against a documented decision, so it is the owner's or the next reader's to make rather than mine to overturn quietly.** The panel's own header argues its case and argues it well: *"`track_stock = 0` means the merchant has told the system not to count, so a number would be fiction"*. That reasoning is right about the NUMBER and silent about the DECLARATION, which is the gap. The fix is small and additive -- fall through to `stock_status` when it says `out_of_stock` or `on_backorder`, keeping "Not tracked" for `in_stock` -- and it would make the panel agree with both the list badge and the cart on the one case where all three can be checked. It is not folded into P2-03-9 because that slice's acceptance is about the LIST's indicator, and because changing a panel P2-03-6 shipped with a stated rationale deserves its own row rather than a line in someone else's. **Severity: low-to-medium** -- the information is available one click away on the list, and the panel is read-only. |
| **F81** | **An unrecognised order status answered with a 500, on three endpoints, and one reachable path needed no crafted request at all.** `Order::order_status_change()` is a chain of `if ($order_status == '...')` arms, each returning. An order that EXISTS with a status matching no arm fell off the end of the method and **returned null** -- and all three callers dereference the result with no guard: `OrderController::order_status_change():1345` (`$responce['status']`), `Api\ApiController:7449` (`$date['status']`) and `Api\DashboardController::statusChange` (`$responce['order_status'] = ...`). The answer was `Trying to access array offset on null`. **MEASURED as a 500** on the web route. The two API callers read the value straight out of the request (`$request->order_status`, `$request->delivered`), so any client can produce it. **AND IT IS REACHABLE WITHOUT A HOSTILE CALLER:** the `pre_order` arm is gated on `module_is_active('PreOrder')`, so a caller sending that perfectly legitimate value -- `orders.delivered_status` 8, which **F29 added to the API's own status vocabulary** -- fell through whenever the module is off, which is the default. | **FIXED [2026-10-26]**, one default return in the shape of the `Order not found.` arm beside it, so the three callers handle it through code they already have. **A return rather than three guards, because nothing is written on this path and that was verified:** the order is read at the top and every arm that touches it returns, so an unmatched status had already changed nothing -- what was wrong was only the REPORT. 7 tests (`OrderStatusUnknownValueTest`), asserting both halves: a clean refusal **and** an untouched row, across four values including the realistic `pre_order`-with-the-module-off case, plus a contract test that the method always returns an array. **Bitten:** removing the default return returns all four cases to 500 and fails the contract test. **A HYPOTHESIS THIS WORK REFUTED, recorded because the method matters more than the conclusion.** The first reading found six handled values by grepping `\$order_status == '[a-z_]+'`, and the order page's dropdown offers `delivered`, which that list did not contain -- which looked like **"a merchant cannot mark an order delivered"**, a serious defect on the most-used merchant screen. Driving all six dropdown values answered 200 and "Delivered": the arm is written `if ($order_status ==  'delivered')` with **two spaces**, and the regex's single space missed it. A grep is a hypothesis, not a finding; the driven measurement is what stopped a false report. A test now asserts all six dropdown values work, so if one ever stops being handled that IS caught. |

The names **do** exist in `edit.blade.php`, in the attribute block that is populated when the merchant touches the attribute picker — not on load. | **CHARACTERISED, NOT FIXED — BLOCKED on owner decision point A.** See `docs/development/phase-2/P2-03-4-VARIANT-PERSISTENCE-DECISION.md` §2. The obvious fix (post the gate's keys) routes **every** ordinary save through a path that deletes variant rows and zeroes prices (F83), which is a change to what a save does to pricing and stock and is reserved by the owner's standing limits. Two safe designs are costed in the document; A1 (edit in place, no rebuild) is recommended. `tests/Feature/Merchant/ProductVariantPersistenceTest.php` asserts the discard so it cannot change unnoticed. **[2026-10-26] THIS DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE. The row above says "the edit page posts NEITHER gate's keys on load".
