And a 66/66 run reported success while two switch captions were visibly dimmer than the fields above them, because the probe checked that labels RESOLVE and never that they can be READ. | **ALL THREE CLOSED AS PREMISES.** Both harnesses now assert the URL they landed on before any other check and skip the rest if it is wrong; the modal is a hard premise with no fallback scope; and the create form's contrast and dialog-overflow are measured, which is what found F126's 2.52:1 and 2.18:1 immediately. The fixture gained the eleven category/attribute permissions — all granted to the `admin` role, a real tenant owner, in `Utility.php:3080ff`, so it moves toward production rather than loosening — plus a CHILD category, without which the select held four flat options and every assertion about the indent passed because there was no indent to get wrong. |
| **F128** | **A LIVE CROSS-TENANT RENAME AND DELETE on `product-attributes/{id}`.** `ProductAttributeController::update` (`:81`) and `::destroy` (`:108`) took a route-model-bound `ProductAttribute` and applied **no ownership predicate** — the only check was `isAbleTo('Edit Attributes')` / `isAbleTo('Delete Attributes')`, which says what the caller may do, never to whose data. So a merchant could rewrite or destroy **another store's** attribute by posting its id. Both permissions are granted to every tenant owner's role by default (`Utility.php:3093-3096`), so the attacker is **any merchant**, not a privileged one. `::edit` (`:72`) had **neither** a permission gate nor a predicate, so any authenticated user could read any store's attribute name. **Nothing else was stopping it, and each was checked rather than assumed:** `ProductAttribute` registers no global scope and is **not** in `config/tenancy.php`'s `scoped_models`, so `StoreScope::isActiveFor()` short-circuits and adds no predicate for any principal — the `Product` shape exactly; the resource route (`web.php:911`) sits in a group carrying only `auth`, `xss`, `setlocate`, `verified`; and `ModuleResourceOwnershipGuard`'s TARGETS map has no attribute entry at all. **The child and the neighbour were both already fixed and the parent was missed** — `ProductAttributeOptionController::ownedAttribute()` is this exact helper, and `CategoryController` carries `categoryIsOutsideCurrentStore()` at its own write sites (P1-INV-08). Found by the independent review of P2-05-4, on the surface that slice converts; no test drove these routes at all. | **FIXED, by the pattern the sibling controller already established** rather than a third shape: a private `ownedAttribute($id)` resolving through `where('id', $id)->where('store_id', getCurrentStore())`, used by all three methods, plus the missing `Edit Attributes` gate on `edit`. An empty store resolves to **null, not to everything** — with `null_tenant => 'open'` a missing tenant adds no predicate, so it is refused explicitly. `Tests\Feature\Security\AttributeOwnershipAndListEscapingTest` drives six cases: a foreign rename, a foreign delete and a foreign read are all refused, the merchant's OWN rename and delete still succeed (so the guard has not simply broken the feature), and a same-store caller without the permission is still refused (so ownership has not replaced the permission with something weaker). Asserted through `withoutGlobalScopes()`, because a plain `find()` answers against whichever tenant resolved rather than against the table. **Revert-checked: 2 failures.** |

'" …>'` by concatenation, and both are in `rawColumns()`. **The filename is attacker-controlled end to end:** `CategoryController:89` builds it from `$request->image->getClientOriginalName()`, which Symfony strips of path separators and nothing else, so a double quote survives; `Utility.php:402` does not sanitise it; `get_file()` returns `url($path)` and Laravel's UrlGenerator rawurlencodes only its `$extra` argument, not the path; and the `XSS` middleware cannot help because it filters only **string** request values, so an `UploadedFile` is skipped. So the control appears exactly when it would work, rather than never. (`@can` with a POLICY is not the `@can('Some Permission')` trap CLAUDE.md warns about: that is always false because `permissions_as_gates => false` unhooks Laratrust from the Gate, which does not affect a real policy ability.) | **F137** | **THE FIRST FIX FOR F136 SHIPPED WITH A TEST THAT COULD NOT FAIL, A GUARD WIDER THAN ANY CALLER NEEDS, AN OPERATOR CHECK THAT DISAGREES WITH THE DATABASE, AND A CALLER-SUPPLIED TENANT PREDICATE LEFT IN THE VIEW.** All four found by the independent authz review of that fix; none by CI. **(1) The vacuous test is the worst of them because it was the positive control.** `test_a_merchant_can_still_read_their_own_user_info` asserted only `assertNotEquals(403, …)` — and `userInfo` has **no 403 path at all**, every refusal being `redirect()->back()`, a 302. It passed on a denial, on a 500, and against the unfixed code. A positive control that cannot fail makes the negative tests beside it look balanced when they are not. **(2) The same-tenant limb was wider than anything needed it.** It admitted any principal whose `creatorId()` matched the target's, so a **zero-permission staff member** could read their own tenant's store counts and, through the view, every colleague's name and login state — while the sibling screens gate exactly that roster on `Manage User` (`UserController:36`, `:805`). It bought nothing: **both** callers are operator-only (`store/index.blade.php:159` inside `@if (Auth::user()->type == 'super admin')` at `:148`; `store/action.blade.php:29` only via `StoreDataTable` for `StoreController::list`, gated the same way), and those are the only two references to the route. **(3) `strtolower(trim($actor->type))` is WIDER THAN THE MySQL COLLATION.** The project's `UserPolicy::isOperator()` deliberately uses `rtrim(..., ' ')` to match PAD SPACE — the engine ignores a trailing space but not a leading one and not a tab — measured and documented at `UserPolicy:74-93`. A row typed `' super admin'` would have been operator to this handler and not-operator to the database. `OperatorCheckCensusTest` does not catch that spelling. **(4) The view still took the route parameter as a tenant predicate.** `compact('id')` passed the RAW parameter and `user_info.blade.php:59` runs `User::where('created_by', $id)`; from the operator's primary button that is CIPHERTEXT, and `users.created_by` is an unsignedBigInteger, so **on MySQL it casts to 0** and the modal lists rows with `created_by = 0` from any tenant. **The suite could never have shown it** — on SQLite the same comparison matches nothing, CLAUDE.md's standing warning about type affinity. | **ALL FOUR FIXED.** The guard is now operator-or-self with no same-tenant limb, through `UserPolicy::isOperator()` rather than a second spelling of the same decision; the view receives `$user->id`; and the test set grew from 5 to **10**. The vacuous control is `assertOk()`, the operator control adds `assertSee()` of the victim's store name (status alone passed on a 500 and on a wrong target), and new cases cover the **raw-id shape** both ways — it is the input surface the fix newly admits, and that caller was a 500 before — the **narrowing** (staff refused on their own tenant's owner), and a route-exists premise so a 404 cannot be mistaken for a refusal. **Bite-tested, all four: guard removed → 3 failures; operator-first dropped → 3; route parameter back in the view → 1; null guard removed → 1.** The MySQL half of (4) still cannot be driven here, and the test that pins it says so in its own docblock: it asserts the FUNCTIONAL consequence that moves in lockstep — the modal listing the target's staff instead of "User not found." |
| **F138** | **Three labelling defects in the operator's Admin Info modal, two of them on switches that decide who can sign in.** `user_info.blade.php` rendered the store switch with a CONSTANT `id="store_disable"` inside a loop — one per store tab, so the document carried duplicate ids and every `for` on the page became ambiguous — and its `<label>` was EMPTY, so the control had no accessible name from any source. The user switch was worse: the input had **no id at all** while its label said `for="user_disable"`, and both repeated per user. That is the control enabling or disabling a staff member's login — the same capability F130 was about. Third, the person's NAME was rendered as `<label for="user">`, a label for an element that does not exist, once per user. | **FIXED** with per-record ids (`store-disable-{id}`, `user-disable-{id}`) and labels naming the store or the user; the name is a `<span>`. Asserted against the **rendered modal** rather than the source, because duplicate ids and a dangling `for` are properties of the document: `UserInfoDisclosureTest` now collects every `id` and every `label[for]` and asserts no duplicates and no dangling targets. **Bite-tested both ways.** |
| **F139** | **The staff list's avatar cell carried three defects at once, all found by the live sweep.** `UserDataTable:28` emitted `<a><img … id="blah3"></a>`: an **anchor with no `href` and no text**, so every row contributed a control with no accessible name (3 on the dev fixture, in all eight configurations); **`id="blah3"` once per row**, so the document carried duplicate ids; and an **image with no `alt`**. The `type` column beside it used `badge bg-primary` — white on the host brand green, the 2.59:1 family. Separately, `roles/permission.blade.php` carried `id="useradd-sidenav"` and `id="pills-tab"` while rendering **once per row**, so both repeated per role — and `pills-tab` additionally COLLIDED with the create-role modal's own `#pills-tab` whenever that modal was open on the same page. | **FIXED.** The avatar is decoration beside the name, so it is a bare `<img>` with an empty `alt` and `aria-hidden` — announced by nothing, which is correct, rather than announced as an unnamed link — with the URL escaped because the cell is built by concatenation in a raw column. The `type` badge goes through `DsBadge`. The two vestigial ids are dropped from the chip partial, after checking that nothing on that screen targets them (the three views that use those selectors are other screens). **Live: 140/140 across eight configurations after the fix.** |
| **F117** | **16 credential fields across 11 settings panels echoed the stored value into the rendered HTML** — live API keys, tokens and mail passwords in the DOM, readable by anyone who can open the page, by any browser extension, and in any screenshot or screen-share of it. Several sit on panels a merchant opens to change something unrelated. | **CLOSED [2026-10-27] — all sixteen converted, display and persistence together, and the ratchet is now EMPTY.** `App\Support\SecretSetting` holds BOTH halves of the rule in one class, and that is the point: `display()` never returns the stored value, `resolve()` keeps it when the submitted box is blank, and a caller using one without the other is the bug the class exists to prevent — these panels write EVERY field on EVERY save, so masking the display over an unconditional `$post[$k] = $request->$k` **erases the credential** on the next save of anything else on the same panel. `<x-ss.secret-field>` is the display half: no value, `type="password"`, `autocomplete="new-password"`, and a hint that says a value is saved and blank will keep it. **Only existence reaches the page — never the value, never the length, and never a partial mask** (a prefix is a meaningful hint for several providers' key formats). **FOUR THINGS THE DIFF ALONE WOULD NOT HAVE SHOWN, each caught by driving the real save path:** (1) the stored value had to be loaded IN the POST handler, because `$setting` is built only in the GET handlers — reading it there would have been an undefined variable resolving to `''`, making the whole fix a silent no-op that still erased; (2) the panels' validators mark the secrets `required` while the integration is enabled, so masked boxes made five panels **impossible to save** until `secretRule()` made the rule `required` only when nothing is stored (first-time setup still demands the value; clearing stays deliberate, through the panel's disable toggle); (3) reCAPTCHA's field posts as `google_recaptcha_secret` and is **stored** as `NOCAPTCHA_SECRET`, so keying the lookup on the field name would have read nothing, resolved to `''`, and silently restored the erase; and (4) `delivery_syria_token` is POSTED TO AN EXTERNAL VERIFIER before it is saved, so resolving only at the save would have sent the empty string and dropped the merchant into "verification failed" against a token that is valid and still stored — it is resolved before the call, and `Http::assertSent` is what proves it. **TWO FIELDS NEEDED A FALLBACK THAT IS NOT `Setting`:** `delivery_syria_token` lives on the USER row (`users.deliverysyria_secret`), and the ChatGPT keys are a repeater in `apikey_setiings` where the stored VALUE used to be the row's identity (`updateOrCreate(['key' => $posted])` then `whereNotIn('key', $posted)->delete()`). A masked box identifies nothing, so each saved key now posts its own row id as `api_key[e<id>]` and a cloned row keeps the bare `api_key[]`; the row id is caller-supplied input, so it is resolved as `where('created_by', $owner)->find($id)` and a forged id reaches nothing. The reconciling delete gained the `created_by` predicate it never had — forced by the masking, not a widening: the form now lists only the caller's rows, so an unscoped delete would wipe every other operator's keys on the first save (**executed against the legacy code: 2 of 2 rows deleted for a filled post**). **AND A MEASURED PREMISE THAT CORRECTED THE WRITTEN RATIONALE:** `ConvertEmptyStringsToNull` means a blank box arrives as **NULL, not `''`**, so the legacy `whereNotIn('key', [null])` matched no row at all and simply stopped reconciling. The first draft of this row and of three docblocks claimed a blank submit would delete every key; it would not, and the claim was corrected against the executed result rather than left as a plausible story. `test_an_empty_box_arrives_as_null…` pins it. **EVIDENCE.** `SecretSettingPersistenceTest` (7) and `SecretSettingNonSettingStoresTest` (11) drive each panel's REAL route; every case carries a WITNESS assertion on a non-secret field, because a refused request looks exactly like a preserved credential — and that witness caught **four** false passes while this was being written (a store with no row, so `getCurrentStore()` was NULL and nothing was written; a Shopify payload using the wrong field name, since `shopify_store_url` is a DNS LABEL under P1-SEC-06, not a URL; a Delivery Syria fixture with no `countries`/`states` rows, so the FK threw, the handler's own `catch` reported a DATABASE error as "Could not connect to verification server" and returned 302 before the save; and a key read through the `Cachable` model that served a row the delete had already removed — the table is read raw now). Each mechanism was **bitten**: reverting the resolve, the permission gate and the repeater sync each turns the matching tests red, and reinstating the exposure turns the now-empty ratchet red. **Unit 442 passed; the two F117 feature classes 18 passed.** **NOT CLAIMED:** a full-suite Feature gate run on this tree — owed at the next integration milestone — and MySQL. SQLite's type affinity hides real MySQL defects; these are string columns and the behaviour is in PHP, but it has not been executed on MySQL and is not claimed to be. **AN INDEPENDENT REVIEW BEFORE COMMIT FOUND FOUR DEFECTS, THREE OF THEM CREATED BY THIS CHANGE, AND THEY ARE THE REASON THIS SLICE IS WORTH READING.** All four are fixed and bitten; the full account is in `PHASE-02-TEST-EVIDENCE.md` §34f. (1) **A staff member could have exfiltrated the store's SMTP password.** A handler that falls back to a stored secret is a handler that will use a secret the caller does not know — harmless on a save path, not on a SEND path, where the caller picks the destination. `testSendMail` merged the resolved `MAIL_PASSWORD` into the request and handed it to `SetConfigEmail()`, which takes `mail.host` from that same request, on a route with no permission check; `mail_host=smtp.attacker.example` with the box left blank would have had the application authenticate to a caller-chosen host with the merchant's credential. **This re-opened P1-SEC-18 F5 through a different door** and no F117 test would have seen it, because every one drove a principal who was allowed. (2) The same shape in `testSendwhatsappmassage`, smaller loss — the Bearer token cannot be redirected off `graph.facebook.com`, so what leaked was use of the merchant's account. Both send handlers and both modals now carry `StoreSettingsAccess::permits()`, the gate on the tab the credential came from. (3) **Three converted panels were left unsaveable** — `mail_password`, `s3_secret` and `wasabi_secret` kept a flat `required` while their boxes were masked, so an ordinary save failed and `keepStoredSecret()` was never reached. Fails closed, erases nothing, but the panel cannot be saved without retyping. This is the defect class the slice had already found and fixed twice, and it still shipped past green because `convertedPanels()` covered **two of the five** converted panels — a data provider is a coverage claim and nothing was checking it. All five now. (4) Two new tests could pass against a refusal, because `ChatgptSettings` returns the same redirect either way; all seven ChatGPT cases now assert the non-secret row the success path writes, and making the handler refuse turns seven of eleven red where four did before. **Not acted on, recorded:** scoping the ChatGPT read to the signed-in operator can show "no value saved" while another operator's key is live and billing — a visibility problem, no principal gains anything, and widening the read would widen the save's blast radius. **SCOPE CORRECTION:** "no credential reaches a settings page" holds for the eleven converted panels and NOT for the payment tab, which still renders every gateway credential into its inputs by deliberate decision; that tab is `permits()`-gated, is not among F117's sixteen, and is not closed by this slice. |

## Where these rows came from

40 of the rows below were created on **2026-10-25** by a requirement-by-requirement audit of the
master prompt against this ledger, recorded in
[`PHASE-02-COVERAGE-AUDIT-2026-10-25.md`](PHASE-02-COVERAGE-AUDIT-2026-10-25.md). They carry
`AUDIT-2026-10-25` in their Decisions column and a `REQUIRED:` note in Verified by, because the
evidence they name does not exist yet.

The audit found **31 of the prompt's 104 workstream requirements tracked by no row at all** —
including Workstream J's Reports and Integrations, and all four of Workstream E's order rules. It
also found the coverage check inside the master-prompt document had gone stale. Nothing was
deleted and no status was changed.

## Scope boundaries — what Phase 02 is NOT

Recorded 2026-10-25 from two sources: boundary sentences inside the master prompt itself, which the
coverage audit surfaced, and the owner's scope-separation instruction of the same date. **These are
not tasks. They exist so that work matching them is recognised and declined rather than absorbed.**

| Boundary | Source | What it means here |
|---|---|---|
| *"Any unsupported advanced order operations belong to Phase 04."* | master prompt, Workstream E closing sentence | An order capability the backend does not already support is NOT a Phase 02 gap. P2-04-11 and P2-04-12 are scoped to *"where supported"* for exactly this reason. |
| *"Do not implement new Phase 04 product pricing or stock engines in Phase 02."* | master prompt, Workstream D | P2-03-8 and P2-03-9 reorganise and label what exists; they add no pricing or stock logic. |
| *"Do not introduce a second competing design system."* / *"Do not replace Laravel Blade with a new frontend framework."* | master prompt, Workstream A | The Store Syria DS and Blade stay authoritative. The vendored design skills **advise**; they do not replace it. |
| **Phase 2.5 — Premium Merchant UI/UX** | owner instruction, 2026-10-25 | A separately approved FUTURE stage. **Must not be silently merged into Phase 02.** Polish beyond the master prompt's own requirements belongs there, not here. |
| **Phase 03 — Storefront, plus a separately scoped Official Website and bilingual Documentation Center** | owner instruction, 2026-10-25 | Larger than the six-phase table's original "Storefront" line. Not started. |
| **Phase 06 — premium Super Admin UX** | owner instruction, 2026-10-25 | Not started. |

**Standing rule:** do not start Phase 2.5, Phase 03 or Phase 06 during Phase 02 execution.

## Remediation slices arising from Phase 02 findings

Not slices of the master prompt — these exist because Phase 02 work **found** them. They are kept
out of the P2-xx numbering so the prompt's own slice list stays what the owner wrote. Owner
instruction, 2026-10-25: *"Do not silently dismiss either finding."*

| ID | Task | Decisions | Status | Files | Verified by | Blocker |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **F46 — the checkout path attributes an order from request input.** `app/Services/PaymentService.php:2689` builds `product_order_id` as `$request->customer_id . date('YmdHis')` for a caller `auth('customers')->check()` has already authenticated, and `:2696` writes the same raw value to `orders.customer_id`. The authenticated customer's own id is available on the line above. `config/database.php:55` sets `'strict' => false` on the `mysql` connection, so MySQL coerces the integer column to 0 and stores the string column whole. <br><br>**Acceptance:** both statements read `auth('customers')->id()`; a shopper cannot attribute an order to another customer; no change to any other behaviour on the checkout path. **NARROWLY SCOPED** — two lines, nothing else in `PaymentService`. | **SECURITY-SENSITIVE, PAYMENT PATH. Owner decision required before the input half is changed** (recorded in `PHASE-02-ACCEPTANCE-REPORT-AR.md` §8.10). The RENDERING half is already fixed and pinned. | **ESCALATED — awaiting owner decision** | `app/Services/PaymentService.php` (not yet changed); rendering half fixed in `app/DataTables/OrderDataTable.php` | **REQUIRED before merge:** a regression test driving a full checkout with a crafted `customer_id`, asserting the order is attributed to the authenticated customer; a cross-customer attempt refused; the existing checkout tests still green. **Already present:** `tests/Feature/Security/OrderListCellEscapingTest.php` pins the rendering half and bites. **NOT measured:** whether a crafted `customer_id` survives a full checkout on production MySQL — reasoned from the column type, the handler and the strict-mode setting, not driven | Owner decision |
| **R2** | **F48 — 29 `env()` calls in host Blade views return NULL under `config:cache`.** Three variables: `env('IS_MOBILE')` x12, `env('APP_URL')` x11, `env('APP_NAME')` x4. Laravel's `env()` reads `$_ENV`, which `php artisan config:cache` does not populate — the normal production posture. Consequences: a HOST-LESS url wherever `APP_URL` is concatenated, a blank application name, and a mobile switch that always reads "desktop". **Production-only, which is why none was noticed.** <br><br>**Acceptance:** every one of the 29 call sites reads `config('app.url')` / `config('app.name')` / a config entry for the mobile switch; a ratchet fails on a new `env()` in a view. | `AUDIT-2026-10-25`; **its own slice** because it crosses many screens; the one call on the order detail page is already fixed under P2-04-6. Census must be keyed on the CALL, not a file list (see handoff §4c) | **DONE — [2026-10-26] swept, measured under `config:cache`, and ratcheted** | `config/platform.php` (new), `tests/Unit/Config/NoEnvCallsInFirstPartyBladeTest.php` (new), 12 view files, `app/Helper/helper.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/LanguageController.php`, `tests/Support/Security/bin/verify-menu-render-parity.php`, both lang files | **THE CENSUS IN THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN TWO WAYS, and its own arithmetic gave it away: 12+11+4 = 27, not 29.** Re-measured keyed on the call: **31 occurrences in `resources/views/`, 30 LIVE** (one is prose inside the `{{-- --}}` comment on `order/view.blade.php:38` that documents this very defect) — `IS_MOBILE` x12, `APP_URL` x10 live, `APP_NAME` x4, and **a fourth variable the row missed entirely, `default_language` x4**. Plus 3 PHP sites that had to move with them or the two halves would disagree under cache: `helper.php:98`, `helper.php:2550` (`IS_MOBILE`) and `LanguageController:386` (`default_language`). `packages/workdo/` left alone (45 calls, vendored — CLAUDE.md). **THE DEFECT IS NO LONGER REASONED, IT IS MEASURED.** With the flags set in `.env`: cached, `env('IS_MOBILE')`=NULL / `config('platform.is_mobile')`='yes'; `env('default_language')`=NULL / `config(...)`='ar'; `env('APP_URL')`=NULL / `config('app.url')`='http://localhost'; `env('APP_NAME')`=NULL. All four were returning NULL in production. **Ratchet:** `NoEnvCallsInFirstPartyBladeTest` — 3 cases, failed before the sweep (28 offender lines) and passes after; **bite-tested on 7 forms** (`env(`, `getenv(`, `Env::get(`, `$_ENV[`, `$_SERVER[`, a call split across lines, and one inside `@php`) with no false positive on `my_env(` or on a Blade comment. **INDEPENDENT REVIEW FOUND FOUR THINGS AND ALL FOUR ARE FIXED:** (1) it caught a break I had shipped — `tests/Support/Security/bin/verify-menu-render-parity.php` **eval**s the real `menuTree()` out of `helper.php` so it cannot drift, and runs with no Laravel, so moving that line to `config()` made it fatal with `Call to undefined function config()`, exit 255, **and no gate would ever have said so** — CI does not run `tests/Support/Security/bin/`. Fixed with a `config()` stub that requires the real config file; the script is back to passing every fixture and selector. (2) `config('app.name')` defaults to `'Laravel'` (`config/app.php:16`), so the `?? 'Ecommercego saas'` fallback would have died and put "Laravel" into a page title, a customer-facing receipt title and a merchant's SMTP test mail — fixed with a `brand_name` key that keeps the shipped brand (verified: APP_NAME absent → 'Ecommercego saas' cached and uncached; APP_NAME=MyShop → 'MyShop'). (3) the sweep changed WHICH language `language/index.blade.php` protects, and `destroyLang()` had **no server-side guard at all** — it computed `$default_lang` and used it only to reassign users, so a crafted URL could delete the default and reassign every affected user to the directory it had just removed; a guard now refuses the default **and** `app.fallback_locale`, bilingual message added. (4) my ratchet reported wrong line numbers (comment-stripping shifted them 56 lines in one file), missed `getenv(`/`Env::get(`/ superglobals, carried a dead variable, and did not scan `themes/` — which `ViewServiceProvider:101,128` renders from (3 themes, 44 blades, currently clean). All four fixed. **NOT measured:** whether this deployment runs `config:cache`; and the vendored twin below | — |

## Slice P2-05 — common merchant screens (Workstream J)

| ID | Task | Decisions | Status | Files | Verified by | Blocker |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2-05-1 | **Four state-changing GETs, two of them unauthorised cross-tenant writes** | — | **DONE** | `ProductBrandController`, `ProductLabelController`, `ProductBrandDataTable`, `ProductLabelDataTable`, `routes/web.php`, 4 views, `database/seed-p2dev.php`, `tests/Feature/Security/{BrandLabelStatusAuthorizationTest,StateChangingGetInventoryTest}.php`, `docs/development/phase-2/tools/check-toggle-posts-live.cjs` | **THE VERB WAS THE SMALLER HALF.** `ProductBrandController::changeStatus`, `changePopular` and `ProductLabelController::changeStatus` were three lines each — `find($request->id)`, assign, save — with **no permission check, no ownership check and no tenant predicate**, and neither model is in `config/tenancy.php`'s `scoped_models`. So **any authenticated web caller could disable or promote any brand, and disable any label, in any store, by walking the id** — and `status` is the column the storefront filters on. **The Flutter siblings were already fixed** (`Api\DashboardController::ChangeStatus` et al. carry `whereIn('store_id', $this->ownedStoreIds())`): somebody closed the API path and left the web path open. All four routes (those three plus `customer-status`, whose handler was already guarded) are now POST. 15 tests; **every control bite-tested separately** — brand store predicate → 3 fail, brand permission → 2, brand operator limb → 1, label store predicate → 1, the disabled-switch gate → 1. Driven in a real browser: **33 cases**, including that each toggle issues a POST, carries no values in the query string, answers 200, **writes the row** (read back after a reload) and **refuses a tokenless `fetch` with 419**. | — |
| P2-05-1a | Ratchet: the set of state-changing GETs cannot grow | — | **DONE** | `tests/Feature/Security/StateChangingGetInventoryTest.php` | **FOUR SWEEPS HAD EACH MISSED ONE.** F12 found `change-store`; its fix was keyed on that route, so F14 found `store/change-status` one screen up the same controller; this slice then found four more. The ratchet walks the real router, keeps host controllers only, reads each handler METHOD'S OWN comment-stripped source for a write, and compares to a declared set **in both directions** — a new one fails as undeclared, a fixed one fails as stale. 90 routes declared and classified: ~48 gateway callbacks (the gateway chooses the verb), 6 Flutter API routes, **8 money/subscription paths including `refund`, `order_approve` and `order_reject` — a refund and a bank-transfer approval, both by GET, both owner decisions**, and the rest needing their own caller sweep. Bite-tested in all three directions. **States its own limits:** a write reached through a helper, a job or an event listener is invisible to the source heuristic, so it is a floor and not a proof. | — |
| P2-05-2 | WS J1 — **Customers screen** on the design system <br><br>**Acceptance:** the list and its row actions use `ss-*` components; every control has an accessible name; AA in LTR/RTL x light/dark; no functionality removed | AUDIT-2026-10-25; splits the old lumped P2-05-2; depends on P2-01-6 | **DONE [2026-10-26]** | — | **REQUIRED:** a live browser check over the screen (pattern: `check-row-actions-live.cjs`) + 4 screenshots; a bite test on any new gate | — `resources/views/customer/{action,status,grid,filter}.blade.php`, `app/DataTables/CustomerDataTable.php`, `tests/Feature/Security/CustomerListOutputEscapingTest.php` (6 tests), `tests/Unit/Merchant/ArabicUiCoverageTest.php` (surface widened), `database/seed-p2dev.php`, `resources/lang/{ar,en}.json` (9 strings), `docs/development/phase-2/tools/{check-row-actions-live,check-customer-delete-live}.cjs` | **ALL FOUR ACCEPTANCE CLAUSES MET AND DRIVEN, in all four configurations: 148/148 with 0 failures** across orders, products and customers (`check-row-actions-live.cjs`, to which `/customer` is now added rather than given a second harness that could drift). The row actions are the DS's own quiet icon buttons; every control has an accessible name that names the **customer** as well as the verb; text reaches 4.5:1 and icons 3:1 in en/ltr and ar/rtl x light and dark; and no functionality was removed — the delete conversion was **driven end to end** (13/13) rather than assumed, asserting that clicking really issues `POST /customer/{id}` and is accepted. **FOUR FINDINGS, and three of them are about measurement rather than markup.** **F101** is the product defect: this screen carried all three faults P2-04-4 fixed on the order list — six unnamed actions per page, an eye icon at **1.68:1** (the identical figure recorded there), and a delete using an inline browser `confirm()` while every other list uses the project's `.show_confirm` SweetAlert handler. **F98** is the security one, found while reading the same screen: `CustomerDataTable` printed shopper-chosen `first_name`, `last_name`, `mobile` and `email` into the merchant's admin page **as markup** — the same stored XSS `OrderDataTable:63-90` already fixed, on the surface that sweep did not reach. Fixed with `e()` per value and by removing `email` from `rawColumns()` entirely. **F102** and **F103** are why the first clean run meant nothing: driving the delete archived all three fixture customers, the seeder could not restore them (`withoutGlobalScopes()` drops the soft-delete scope, so the create was skipped forever), and the harness then reported names and contrast as **PASSING on an empty table**. The harness also counted rows from a hidden second table, failing the delete assertion `3 >= 6` on a screen where every row does have one. Both premises are now strict. **F100** records the census this produced: 16 other DataTables share F98's shape, triaged by who writes the value — most are self-XSS, but `NewsletterDataTable` and `TestimonialDataTable` are shopper→merchant and are the named next candidates. **NOT claimed:** the `@permission`-gated controls were not re-examined for the platform operator, who `isAbleTo()` refuses — that question spans 64 views and is recorded rather than answered here. |
| P2-05-3 | WS J2 — **Inventory / stock views** on the design system <br><br>**Acceptance:** stock figures carry their rule (the product list's own threshold predicate, see P2-02-3); AA in all four configurations; no functionality removed | AUDIT-2026-10-25; depends on P2-01-6 | **DONE [2026-10-26]** | Four defects found and fixed on this one screen, all measured live — three recorded as **F106** (the status column empty on two of three tabs and reading the wrong setting; the row actions with no accessible name; the active tab pill at 2.59:1) and a fourth as **F107** (the action column entirely outside the visible box at scroll 0 in all eight configurations). **Acceptance, condition by condition.** (1) *Stock figures carry their rule*: the state is computed once by `ReportController::withStockState()` from **`ProductDataTable::trackedStockState()`** — the same function the catalogue's badge and filter use — so the three tabs cannot disagree with the product list, and the rule is in each badge's `title`. (2) *AA in all four configurations*: the active pill measures **5.36:1** in ltr/light, ltr/dark, rtl/light and rtl/dark, from the same run that produced the screenshots; the full-page sweep reports 84 measured, 0 below AA. (3) *No functionality removed*: `stock_status` stays in the payload because the datatables column definitions still reference it, and the row action still navigates — proven by a real click, not by geometry. | **MET.** 12 screenshots + README in `docs/development/phase-2/evidence/p2-05-3/`, regenerable by `docs/development/phase-2/tools/shoot-stock-report-live.cjs`; the predicate asserted against `ProductDataTable::trackedStockState()` **called directly** by `tests/Feature/Merchant/StockReportPredicateTest.php` (10 tests). | **ONE THING IS DELIBERATELY LEFT BEHIND AND MUST NOT BE READ AS FIXED:** the tab rail is shared with `module/index`, `country/index` and `language/index`, which still carry the same 2.59:1 active pill. The AA fix is scoped to this screen by a new `ss-tabrail` class; the other three belong to other slices and are recorded in F106. |

| P2-05-5 | WS J4 — **Staff and permissions** on the design system <br><br>**Acceptance:** users and roles screens use `ss-*`; permission names rendered are ones that exist (the P2-02-13 trap); AA in all four | AUDIT-2026-10-25; depends on P2-01-6; interacts with `RolePolicy` | **DONE [2026-10-27]** | `resources/views/users/{action,index,list,create,edit,reset,profile,user_info}.blade.php`, `resources/views/roles/{action,index,create,permission}.blade.php`, `app/DataTables/UserDataTable.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/UserController.php`, `tests/Unit/Dashboard/DashboardPermissionNamesTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Ui/BladeViewsCompileTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Merchant/StaffAndRoleRowActionsTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Security/UserInfoDisclosureTest.php`, `database/seed-p2dev.php`, `docs/development/phase-2/tools/shoot-staff-roles-live.cjs`, `docs/development/phase-2/evidence/p2-05-5/` | **REQUIRED:** live browser check + 4 screenshots; a test that every permission name the screens gate on is grantable (pattern: `DashboardPermissionNamesTest`) | **ALL ACCEPTANCE CLAUSES MET AND DRIVEN: 140/140 across EIGHT configurations (en/ar x light/dark x 1440/390), 16 screenshots, plus 348/348 on `check-row-actions-live.cjs` with both screens added to it.** The acceptance names the P2-02-13 trap and the trap was present (**F130**). Seven findings in all: F130 (a control gated on a permission seeded nowhere, hidden from every principal), F131 (five unnamed, ungated row actions whose four handlers disagree), F132 and F134 (labels pointing at ids that do not exist — twice, by two different mechanisms, the second 13 of 59 labels in the permission matrix), F133 (chips at 2.59:1 that were also fake links), F135 (a Blade COMMENT that 500s the screen, now ratcheted by `BladeViewsCompileTest`), F136/F137 (a cross-tenant disclosure found while converting `user_info`, and four corrections to its own first fix), F138 and F139. **Responsive and accessibility are evidenced rather than asserted:** no horizontal page overflow at 390px, every `for` resolves, no id is emitted twice, every visible control has an accessible name resolved in accname order, and AA over the effective background. **NOT CLAIMED:** `users/{profile,user_info}` are converted but are not in the screenshots (a page the harness does not visit, and a modal); their labelling is covered by `UserInfoDisclosureTest` against the rendered modal. | P2-05-6 | WS J5 — **Reports** already supported by the backend <br><br>**Acceptance:** the report screens under `resources/views/reports/` are reachable, gated on `Manage Reports` / `Manage Order Reports` / `Manage Stock Reports`, on the design system, and every figure carries its period and its qualification (PRD-001) | AUDIT-2026-10-25; **NAMED IN WS J AND PRESENT IN NO ROW BEFORE THIS AUDIT**; depends on P2-01-6 | **PARTIAL — hub DONE [2026-10-27]** | — | **REQUIRED:** live browser check + 4 screenshots; a test that each report screen refuses a principal lacking its permission; MySQL-only SQL noted in B5 must not be exercised on SQLite | — **[2026-10-27] THE GATING HALF OF THIS ROW'S ACCEPTANCE WAS ALREADY TRUE and I nearly re-reported it as missing.** A grep of `ReportController` for `isAbleTo|permits|abort(|super admin` returns ZERO across 3313 lines, and the routes carry only `auth, xss, setlocate, verified` — which reads exactly like an ungated controller. It is not: P1-SEC-09 gates all 29 actions through `App\Http\Middleware\ReportPermission`, attached in the constructor, with operator-first ordering, an unmapped-action fallback to `Manage Reports`, and `ReportAuthorizationTest` (54 cases) including a census that every routed action is mapped. **A source-text check is only as wide as the spellings it knows** — the third time this phase has recorded that, and the first where it would have produced a false FINDING rather than a missed one. **THE HUB IS DONE.** `reports/index.blade.php` + `reports/chart_data.blade.php` are on the design system, and PRD-001 holds on them: `chart_data` rendered `{{ $totaluser }} orders` — a bare number over three charts — with the selected window living only in a CSS class on a button at the top of the page. Four `<x-ss.stat>`s now carry the period, derived once in `ReportController::chartPeriodLabels()` from the same input the data was built from, so the label and the data cannot drift. **The three ApexCharts had no accessible representation of any kind** — no table, no summary, not even a `role="img"` label, so a screen-reader user got three empty divs; they are now two `<x-ss.bar-chart>`s whose numbers ship as real tables with captions and `scope` on both axes, which also removes a JS charting dependency from this screen. The period buttons were `<button class="nav-link">` inside a `<ul role="tablist">` with **no `role="tab"` and no `aria-selected`** (selection conveyed by a CSS class alone) and with two `<div>`s as direct children of the `<ul>` (invalid); they are `aria-pressed` chips, and the JS reads `aria-pressed` rather than a parallel class so the announced and visible states cannot diverge. The date field had no label. The CSV export's header row was hardcoded English. **THE COMPONENT GREW A SECOND SERIES**, additively: `values2`/`valueLabel2`/`columnLabel`, one shared scale across the stack (scaling each series to its own maximum would draw a 3 and a 4 at the same height), and `--ss-chart-series-1/2` as real tokens in all three theme blocks — a teal/gold pair that separates by hue AND lightness, so deuteranopia and protanopia can tell the series apart. `BarChartComponentTest` spends half its assertions proving the single-series path is byte-for-byte unchanged, because the dashboard's chart is covered by browser checks that no PHP run would have caught regressing. **THE MYSQL LIMIT IS REAL AND NOT PAPERED OVER.** `ReportController` uses raw `MONTH()`, `YEAR()` and `DATE()` 36 times, so FOUR of the five windows — year, last-month, this-month and the picked date/range — raise "no such function: MONTH" on SQLite and **cannot be rendered in the suite at all**; only `seven-day` is driven end to end. Shimming the functions would have made all five green and turned the test into a test of the shim. Instead the PRD-001 property, which is a pure function of the request, is covered for all five in `tests/Unit/Ui/ReportPeriodLabelTest.php` (7 cases, no database). The split is deliberate and each half catches what the other cannot: **bite-testing proved it** — hard-coding the label to "Last 7 days" leaves the FEATURE test green (its one window is the one that was hard-coded) and turns all 7 unit cases red. **Arabic:** `resources/views/reports/*` was not in `ArabicUiCoverageTest::SURFACES` at all, so nothing had ever checked these screens; it is now, and the 19 new strings are in `ar.json` and `en.json` (the second ratchet, `TranslationFileIntegrityTest`, caught them as orphans). **Unit 454 passed; DesignSystem + reports + merchant 240 passed.** **STILL TODO on this row:** the other ten report screens (order, bar-chart order, order status, order country, sale-by product/category/brand, top-5, downloadable, stock) and their twelve partials; the live browser check and 4 screenshots, deferred so one browser run can cover several screens rather than one run per slice. **[2026-10-27] ORDER STATUS REPORT DONE, and it was counting SEVEN OF THE NINE STATUSES.** `showOrderStatusReport` built a fresh literal map `0 => 'Pending' … 6 => 'Shipped'` while `delivered_status` carries nine values — `ORDER_STATUS_PARTIALLY_PAID` (7) and `ORDER_STATUS_PRE_ORDER` (8) were simply absent, so for any store that uses either, all three charts showed a breakdown whose parts did not sum to the store's order count and **nothing on the screen said so**: there was no total to compare against, which is exactly what made it invisible. This is the same defect `HomeController::getOrderCounts()` had already fixed for the dashboard (six of nine, plus an `other` catch-all so the parts always sum) — found and fixed twice, independently, because the two screens each wrote their own map. The report now takes its labels from HomeController's constants, carries the `other` catch-all, and states the total on the card. `OrderStatusReportHonestyTest` asserts the IDENTITY (sum == total) rather than individual numbers, because a test that checked "the Pre Order row exists" passes against a map that lists it and counts it wrong. **THE SCREEN STATED NO PERIOD AND THE QUERY HAS NO DATE FILTER** — it is every order the store has ever taken, reading as though it were current. It says "All time". **Three cards carried the IDENTICAL heading** "Order Status Report" over three ApexCharts of the same nine numbers, none with any accessible representation; the AREA chart drew categories as a continuous quantity, so the line sloping from "Cancel" to "Return" claimed something exists between them. One card, one `<x-ss.bar-chart>`, numbers in a real table. The PDF export now opens the `<details>` before capture and closes it after, or the PDF would be bars with no values. **THE WORDING OF STATUS 2 IS DELIBERATELY LEFT WRONG.** `__('Cancel')` is the button word, not the state (the same defect P2-07-6 fixed in the Flutter app), but `Order::getDeliveredStatusStringAttribute()` says `Cancel` and `tests/Feature/ApiContract/OrderStatusTextTest.php` PINS it for the app. Using the better word in the report alone would make it disagree with the order list and with the app, so it is a contract question recorded here rather than a change made here. **TWO ARABIC STATUSES WERE THE WRONG SENSE** and are corrected, since every use of both keys is an order status: `Return` was `العودة` ("the going back") → `مُرتجع`; `Confirmed` was `تأكيد` (the noun "confirmation") → `مؤكَّد`. Same class as the recorded "Stock"→"الأسهم". **And the `<tfoot>` total has now been miscounted as data three times** in this phase — twice in `BarChartComponentTest` and once here, where it made every sum come out at exactly double. The row scan is scoped to `<tbody>` and says why. **Unit 454 passed; DesignSystem + report authz + ApiContract + Commerce 760 passed, 7 skipped.** Bitten: restoring the seven-status map turns 3 of the 7 new cases red. |

## Slice P2-03 — products (Workstream D)

| ID | Task | Decisions | Status | Files | Verified by | Blocker |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2-03-1 | Product list redesign (**row actions done** under P2-04-4's sweep — see F38/F39/F40) | UX-001 | **DONE — [2026-10-26] the row-density half that kept this PARTIAL is closed under P2-03-10** | `app/Helper/helper.php` (`buildDataTable`, `buildDataTableColumn`), `resources/views/components/datatable.blade.php`, `public/css/store-syria-ds.css`, `docs/development/phase-2/tools/check-pinned-column-live.cjs`, `docs/development/phase-2/evidence/p2-03-1/` | **THE MEASUREMENT CAME FIRST AND IT MOVED THE SLICE.** The product list is a FIXED width at every viewport — 1250px in English, 1168px in Arabic, eleven columns, measured identical at 1440, 1280, 992, 768 and 390. `.table-responsive` contains that, so **the document does not overflow at any of the five widths** and the original note that it "overflows its wrapper" implied a page-level scrollbar that is not there. What it costs instead is reaching the end of a row, and the honest number is the one that surprised: with the pinned class removed, a real click on Edit at the table's starting scroll position **navigated nowhere in all eight configurations — 1440px included**, where the table overflows its wrapper by 155px (en) and 73px (ar). The row actions were off-screen on a DESKTOP, not only on a phone. **TWO CHANGES, BOTH IN ONE PLACE.** (1) The column-visibility menu: `public/vendor/datatables/buttons.colVis.min.js` was **already loaded on every DataTable page and unused** — measured, `$.fn.dataTable.ext.buttons.colvis` resolved while only Reset and Reload rendered. Adding it to `buildDataTable` reaches all 68 tables with **no `packages/workdo/` edit**, and the existing `stateSave` (P2-06-4) makes the choice a preference rather than a per-visit toggle. `ss-col-locked` keeps the actions column and the hidden primary key out of the menu. (2) The actions column is `position: sticky` at the **inline end**, which flips on its own for Arabic. **THE PINNED CELL'S COLOUR IS THE ugly part and is stated as such in the CSS.** A sticky cell needs an opaque background; `var(--ss-surface)` is WRONG in dark mode (the host card is `#292a33`, the token `#263331` — different hues, a visible band: ledger F8 again), and `background-color: inherit` chained from the card **does not reach** because DataTables injects three divs of its own between the wrapper and the table — tried, and the cell still computed `rgba(0,0,0,0)`. So it is a custom property carrying a COPY OF A VENDORED COLOUR, **ratcheted** by the check rather than trusted. `check-pinned-column-live.cjs` — **55 cases**: the menu omits both locked columns, a toggle hides a column and survives a reload, and in all four LTR/RTL × light/dark configurations the cell is sticky, holds the correct edge, keeps its heading over it, is opaque, **matches the card's own painted colour**, draws its edge on the scrolled-under side, and **Edit is clickable with no sideways scrolling**. The button's own accessible name is three of those cases and was a defect found by checking: DataTables Buttons **1.3.1 silently ignores the `attr` config** that would set it (rendered and measured — `aria-label` and `role` both null), so the button was named only by `title`, the last-resort source this codebase has already been bitten by in F38/F39. It is now set from `initComplete`, and the case asserts the name is **Arabic**, not merely non-empty — `Choose columns` was missing from both language files, so a non-empty assertion would have passed over an English name on an Arabic screen, which is ledger P2-03-7 exactly. Each leg asserts its own premise (the right stylesheet really loaded; the table really is wider than its wrapper; the scroll really moved). **Bite-tested seven times, each revert confirmed by re-reading the file:** dropping `ss-col-pinned` → 6 failures; dropping `$colvisButtonConfig` → 5; dropping `ss-col-locked` → 2; setting the dark background to `var(--ss-surface)` → exactly the 2 dark colour-match cases, printing `cell=rgb(38,51,49) card=rgb(41,42,51)`, which is the measurement behind the paragraph above; dropping the `initComplete` naming → 2; deleting the Arabic `Choose columns` key → exactly the translation case, printing `aria-label="Choose columns"`. **The check was fixed twice by its own bites**: it first THREW on the missing selector instead of naming a case, and the reachability case was being skipped by the `continue` that fires exactly when the class is absent — so it went silent on its most important claim at the moment the claim stopped holding. Unit 370 passed. Feature at the declared outcome set. The four other live checks re-run unchanged (table-prefs 21, row-actions 108, list-filters 112, order-detail 112, toggle-posts 33) and contrast 76 readings, 0 below AA. 8 screenshots in `evidence/p2-03-1/`, regenerable by the committed `tools/shoot-product-list-live.cjs`. **17 existing screenshots from P2-03-2 and P2-04 are also updated in this commit** — the live checks re-shoot them, and they now carry the pinned column and no longer carry the Laravel Debugbar, which had been sitting across the bottom of every one of them. Spot-checked that each still shows what its own slice claims (the applied stock filter still narrows to its 4 rows). **NOT DONE, and the row stays PARTIAL for it: row density.** Rows are 55px at every viewport and there is no compact option; that needs a control and a persisted preference of its own. Pinning the actions does not make a 1250px table narrow — it makes its actions reachable, and that is all this row claims. **[2026-10-25] THE VERIFICATION DEBT IS PAID, and paying it found two defects — both in the harness, neither in the product.** `check-pinned-column-live.cjs` now reports **58/58** at `6e3564e5` against a correctly seeded and module-enabled instance. First run was 57/58. (1) The Arabic accessible-name case was **order-dependent**: `setServerState()` writes `cust_darklayout` and `SITE_RTL` only, so the menu block flipped the DIRECTION without the LANGUAGE, and `__('Choose columns')` resolves against `users.language` — a column `/change-language/<lang>` PERSISTS. With `users.language='en'` (fresh seed) 1/58 FAILED; with it left `'ar'` by a previous run of this script's own rtl leg, 58/58 passed. Same commit, opposite verdicts. Cases 5-7 already set the language per leg; the menu block did not. Fixed there, bite-tested both ways. The label itself was never wrong — `resources/lang/ar.json:3004` has carried it since `567686f0` and it renders as `اختيار الأعمدة`. (2) `seed-p2dev.php` passed `'lang' => 'en'` to a column that **does not exist** — it is `users.language`, and `'lang'` is not in `User::$fillable`, so mass assignment silently dropped it (measured: `'lang'=>'xx'` → `'en'`, `'language'=>'xx'` → `'xx'`). Harmless today because the default is `'en'`, but it left the language the menu block depends on unpinned. **The BulkDelete premise is now satisfiable:** `database/enable-p2dev-modules.php` enables the module through the two rows production uses, and handoff §6 carries the step. Both rows are needed — with `add_on_managers.is_enable=1` alone the premise still failed `count=0`, because `module_is_active()` for a type `admin` merchant reads `user_active_modules`. Suites at this HEAD: Unit **372 passed**, Feature **5921 passed / 2 declared failures / 17 declared skips, gate OK**, Contract **26 passed**. Evidence: `PHASE-02-TEST-EVIDENCE.md` §10. **STILL NOT DONE: row density (P2-03-10).** | — |
| P2-03-2 | Product list filters | UX, G | **DONE** (saved views remain, with P2-04-8) | `app/DataTables/ProductDataTable.php`, `product/partials/filters.blade.php`, `product/index.blade.php`, `ProductController::index`, `public/css/store-syria-ds.css`, `resources/lang/{ar,en}.json`, `docs/development/phase-2/tools/check-product-filters-live.cjs` | **THE LIST HAD NO FILTERS AT ALL** — only DataTables' free-text box — so a merchant could not ask the one question they actually ask, "what is out of stock", while the dashboard shows that count and links here. Status, stock, category and variants, **with no JavaScript**: the bar is a GET form and yajra's `minifiedAjax()` sets `ajax.url` to the CURRENT url, so both legs see the same parameters — measured in a browser before the design relied on it. `ss_`-prefixed names, because DataTables' own `draw`/`columns`/`search`/`order`/`start`/`length` travel in that same query string. **A FILTER MAY ONLY NARROW:** the store predicate is applied first and filters are additional predicates; an unrecognised value adds none. `ProductListFilterTest` — 12 cases including that the three stock filters **partition** the tracked products on boundary values, that an untracked product is not "out of stock" (its zero is the absence of a fact), that 14 filter values including SQL fragments cannot reach another tenant, and that **no value returns more rows than no filter**. Bite-tested: the store predicate → 1 fail, the `track_stock` guard → 1, the low-stock boundary made exclusive → 1. **58 cases in a real browser across LTR/RTL × light/dark**, including that the AJAX leg inherits the filter and that clearing restores the full count. Contrast: 76 readings, 0 below AA. | — |
| P2-03-3 | Full-page product editor | UX | **PARTIAL — [2026-10-26] the SAVE AFFORDANCES and the basic/advanced field organisation (P2-03-8) are in on both create and edit. END-TO-END SAVE IS NOW PROVEN IN BOTH DIRECTIONS: update, and — since F58 was approved and fixed — CREATE, with a cover image and a gallery image really uploaded. Scrolled to the bottom of the create form it sits at **y = -1175 at 1440px** and **y = -3131 at 390px** — entirely off-screen, so a merchant who has just filled the form cannot reach Save without scrolling back over three thousand pixels on a phone. **THE NEW BUTTON DOES NOT SUBMIT.** `create.blade.php` binds ~120 lines of FormData assembly to `#submit-all`'s click, and `edit.blade.php`'s includes the `ss_last_seen_at` concurrency token from P2-03-5. So the row is `32 + 2*padding + 1px border` (the `td` is `content-box`): comfortable **55px**, compact **41px**. The 32px control is deliberately untouched, which keeps the acceptance's 24px touch target with 8px to spare; shrinking it to win pixels would spend the requirement to buy rows. **MY FIRST ARITHMETIC SAID 40px AND THE BROWSER SAID 41** — the measurement was right and the sum was short a border, which is why the expectation was corrected rather than the code. **IT RIDES THE STATE THAT ALREADY EXISTS:** P2-06-4's `stateSave`/`stateDuration: 0` persists whatever keys sit on the state object, so density is one more key beside the column set and the page length — same store, same no-expiry, no second preference mechanism. **A REAL INIT-ORDER BUG WAS FOUND AND FIXED BY THE CHECK:** DataTables saves state DURING init, BEFORE `initComplete` runs, so a save that read `data-ss-density` off the container read nothing and **stored `comfortable` over a stored `compact`**. The DOM looked right for that page view and the NEXT reload came back comfortable — the shape of bug a careless manual test passes. A per-table map is now the source of truth and the DOM its projection. **Comfortable is the ABSENCE of the attribute**, so the stylesheet has only a compact rule and a table nobody has touched renders byte-for-byte as before. The pressed state is an inset ring, not a colour, so it survives a merchant's brand colour and forced colours. **EVIDENCE:** `check-row-density-live.cjs` **38/38**, bitten three ways (remove the CSS rule → 8 fail; save reads the DOM instead of the map → 2 fail, reproducing the original defect exactly; drop `aria-pressed` → 3 fail). `RowDensityTest` 4 tests / 24 assertions, bitten two ways. 8 screenshots, both densities × LTR/RTL × light/dark, each stamped with its measured row height. ## Slice P2-06 — search, notifications, preferences, onboarding (Workstreams G, H, I)

| ID | Task | Decisions | Status | Files | Verified by | Blocker |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2-06-1 | Onboarding: getting-started checklist | UX | **DONE — SHIPPED** | `components/ss/setup-checklist.blade.php`, `public/css/store-syria-ds.css`, `resources/lang/{ar,en}.json` | Correct in LTR; permission filtering, counter and link/text behaviour are component logic and unit-visible | **An RTL paint anomaly I could not resolve or attribute.** Each step's mark renders TWICE under `dir="rtl"` in the preview harness, once correctly and once at the far inline-end. Measured: the DOM holds exactly one mark per row; `getClientRects()` returns one rect; no shadow, transform, filter or pseudo-element; only `box-sizing` rules match it besides its own; hiding the mark removes both circles; **LTR renders one**. Unchanged by icon-font → CSS-border → inline-SVG, by `position` static/relative, by flex → grid, by `overflow: hidden`, and by removing the label's `flex`. That exhausts the harness. The remaining candidate is the harness's own RTL layout (it loads `style-rtl.css` + `rtl-custom.css` into a stand-in), and separating the two needs the real application — i.e. the Composer blocker. **Not rendered on the dashboard until then**: the wiring is written and commented out in `dashboard/overview.blade.php`, so shipping it is a one-block change once it can be seen in the real app. |
| P2-06-2 | Permission-aware global search | G | **DONE** | `app/Http/Controllers/MerchantSearchController.php`, `partision/command-palette.blade.php`, `public/js/store-syria-ds.js`, `routes/web.php` | `MerchantSearchAuthorizationTest` — **11 cases**. Explicit `store_id` predicate in every query (Product and Order carry no effective scope; Customer is scoped and gets one anyway), operator-first per-section permissions, a section the caller may not see is ABSENT not empty, unresolved tenant refused, LIKE wildcards stripped before the length gate, and a non-string `q` (`?q[]=a`). **BITE TEST, RE-MEASURED AND CORRECTED.** An earlier version of this row — and of the note in the test file — said no endpoint case fails on a single control being removed. That was wrong. Removing each predicate in turn: products → `⨯ a merchant does not find another tenants products` (1 failed, 10 passed); orders → `⨯ a merchant does not find another tenants orders` (1 failed, 10 passed); customers → 11 passed. So **products and orders DO bite on a single removal** (neither model is in `scoped_models`, so nothing covers for the predicate); **customers is the one section that does not**, because `Customer`'s global scope supplies the same predicate for a principal who resolves a tenant. The explicit predicate stays there on the project's rule that the scope must never be the only tenant check, and the hazard is pinned by its own direct-query test. Driven in a real browser across 8 combinations. | — |
| P2-06-2-OLD | (superseded row) | G | — | — | — | Server-side work: tenant isolation, permission and module-entitlement enforcement, none of which can be runtime-verified while Composer is blocked |
| P2-06-3 | Unified notifications | H | **DONE** | `app/Http/Controllers/MerchantNotificationController.php`, `resources/views/notification/index.blade.php`, `resources/views/partision/header.blade.php` (the bell), `public/css/store-syria-ds.css`, `public/js/store-syria-ds.js` (`updateNotificationBadge`), `routes/web.php`, `resources/lang/{ar,en}.json`, `docs/development/phase-2/tools/shoot-notifications-live.cjs` | **THE ROWS ALREADY EXISTED AND THE WEB COULD NOT SEE THEM.** `store_notifications` is written by `StoreNotificationService` on every order created and paid, and the shipped Flutter app reads them through `GET api/admin/notification`. Nothing in the web admin read them at all — a merchant at a desk had no notifications and a merchant with the app had them. Closed against the SAME table, **no schema change, and the API untouched**. `MerchantNotificationAuthorizationTest` — **17 cases**: four handlers, two of which write; cross-tenant on all four; the route ACTIONS asserted (a module route file loads after `routes/web.php` and would win the slot silently); the marks refuse GET (405, having moved nothing); per-TYPE permission, because a notification is not a neutral envelope — `order_created`'s message names the order and its `data` carries `final_price`; the platform operator admitted while holding no Laratrust permission; an unregistered type refused even to a merchant holding everything; unresolved tenant; non-integer id; and the unread count. **Bite-tested, eight controls separately:** store predicate → 5 fail; `whereIn('type')` → 1; the scoped find in `markRead` → 1; the operator limb → 1; the type registry → 1; the mass-write store limb → 2; the bell's placement → 1; the `Show Order` button gate → 1. Three of those cases exist BECAUSE a bite round found nothing — no test drove an operator, the type filter was redundant with the empty-types short-circuit, and the unresolved-tenant case could not fail at all. **THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW FOUND TWO LIVE DEFECTS HERE AND FOUR WEAK TESTS; all six are fixed and every fix is bite-tested** — rows F23–F26 below. Driven in a real browser across 8 combinations, including that the unread marker MIRRORS (`border-inline-start`: left in LTR, right in RTL, read off the painted box). | — |
| P2-06-4 | Persisted table preferences | I | **DONE** | `app/Helper/helper.php` (`buildDataTable`), `public/js/store-syria-ds.js` (`sanitizeTableState`), `docs/development/phase-2/tools/{check-table-state.cjs,check-table-prefs-live.cjs}`, `database/seed-p2dev.php` | **ONE CHANGE, 68 TABLES** — `buildDataTable()` builds every DataTable in the application (39 in `app/DataTables/`, 29 inside vendored modules), so the option goes there and **no `packages/workdo/` file is touched** to reach the vendored twenty-nine. `stateSave` with `stateDuration => 0` (localStorage, no expiry — the DataTables default of 7200 expires the choice after two hours and `-1` is sessionStorage; neither is a preference). **KEPT:** rows per page, sort, column visibility. **DROPPED, both on save and on load:** the search term and the page number — restoring a search is what turns this into a support ticket, and a state written by an earlier build is already in localStorage, which is why the load hook is not redundant. No schema change; what is stored is a row count, a sort direction and booleans, with no tenant or customer data. Evidence in three layers: `TablePreferencesTest` (8 cases — the wiring, that yajra emits the hooks as REAL JS and not quoted strings, the absent-script guard, and three real list screens still rendering); `check-table-state.cjs` (16 cases, the sanitiser EXECUTED in node against DataTables 1.10.20's own state shape); `check-table-prefs-live.cjs` (21 cases in a real browser). | — |

| P2-06-10 | WS D9 / I2 — **Contextual field help** <br><br>**Acceptance:** help attached to the field it is about via `aria-describedby`, in both languages, not a tooltip only | AUDIT-2026-10-25; `x-ss.help` and `.ss-field__hint` exist (see the P2-03-2 layout lesson) | **DONE [2026-10-26] on the product editor; the other screens remain** | `resources/views/components/ss/field-help.blade.php` (new), `resources/views/product/{create,edit}.blade.php` (5 fields each), `resources/lang/{en,ar}.json` (5 strings), `tests/Feature/Merchant/ContextualFieldHelpTest.php` (7 tests), `docs/development/phase-2/tools/check-field-help-live.cjs` (57 cases), `docs/development/phase-2/tools/set-stock-management.php` (new), `measure-contrast-live.cjs` | **WHAT WAS THERE BEFORE IS WHY THE ACCEPTANCE SAYS "NOT A TOOLTIP ONLY".** The slug field carried `'data-bs-toggle' => 'tooltip', 'title' => __('Sku or Slug')` -- hover-only, a restatement of its own label, and bound to the input for nothing: Bootstrap's tooltip plugin moves `title` into `data-bs-original-title` and blanks the attribute (`order/action.blade.php:8` records the same behaviour), so even the browser's own fallback is gone. **No other field in the product editor had help at all.** Five fields now carry real help, each stating something the label does not and each VERIFIED AGAINST THE CODE rather than written from intuition: `slug` (auto-generated from the name when blank -- `store():286`), `sale_price` (must be **lower than** the price -- the rule is `lt:price`, not `lte`), `cost_price` (`required\|numeric\|min:0\|lte:price`, and never shown to customers), `product_weight` (shipping), and `low_stock_threshold` (what makes the list show **Low stock** -- which ties it to P2-03-9's badge, and says that 0 means "use the store default", the `NULLIF(…, 0)` semantics the filter's SQL uses). **THE RATCHET ASSERTS BOTH DIRECTIONS OF THE ASSOCIATION, because each one alone is satisfiable by something broken:** "five hints exist" passes with five paragraphs nothing points at, and "five controls have `aria-describedby`" passes with five references to ids that are not on the page -- and a dangling reference is **worse than silence**, because it leaves the accessible description empty while the markup looks complete. 7 tests, **bitten six ways**, each failing exactly its own cases: dropping one `aria-describedby` (which also makes that hint an orphan, so two cases fail), removing a hint and leaving its reference, adding an orphan hint, restoring the useless tooltip, copying the English text into `ar.json`, and removing the help from `edit` only. **DRIVEN LIVE 57/57**, resolving the id chain the way the accessibility tree does -- so it catches what the Feature test cannot: a reference to a missing id (measured: the description comes back **empty**) and a **duplicate id**, where the browser silently describes the control with the first match. Both bitten. En/LTR/light and ar/RTL/dark, with every description confirmed to be in Arabic in the Arabic run. **CONTRAST: 4 configurations, 2 new targets, 5.23–7.11:1, and 56 measured with 0 below AA.** The weight field's help sits INSIDE the P2-03-8 collapsed `<details>`, a different surface again -- the distinction that has cost a measurement three times now (F8, the feed timestamp, F75's gallery hint). **A PRECONDITION THAT IS STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** `low_stock_threshold` lives inside `@if ($stock_management == 'on')`, so with the setting unset -- how it ships -- the field is not on the page. The first version of the test reported it as unhelped, which was true of the page and false of the template. Both the test and the harness now turn the setting on and **assert the field is present** before judging its help. The harness does it through a new `set-stock-management.php` rather than the fixture, because enabling it in `seed-p2dev.php` would change the editor's rendered field set -- which `inventory-product-fields.cjs` diffs as its preservation gate. That script resolves `settings.created_by` **from the store** (measured: owner 2, not 1), the same way `set-preview-state.php` does; hardcoding 1 would have written the setting where the store never reads it. | **The product editor only.** WS I2's wording is "contextual field help", and the other merchant screens -- orders, customers, inventory, the ~20 settings panels -- have none. The component and the ratchet are reusable, and the ratchet's field list is per-screen, so extending it is additive. Not claimed as complete for Workstream I |
